#### Solved PYQ 1 — 2024 GS-IV, Q2(a) — 10 marks — 150 words — **directly owned**

**Question (verbatim official English wording):** "It is not enough to talk about peace, one must believe in it; and it is not enough to believe in it is one must act upon it." In the present context, the major weapon industries of the developed nations are adversely influencing continuation of number of wars for their own self-interest, all around the world. What are the ethical considerations of the powerful nations in today's international arena to stop continuation of ongoing conflicts.

**Source note:** exact English text from the locally held official PDF `books\mains\05 UPSC 2024 Paper-IV_Final 1.pdf`, question 2(a), page 2. The printed source itself carries the ungrammatical clause "and it is not enough to believe in it is one must act upon it"; the artefact is preserved rather than silently corrected.

**Ownership note.** ✅ The 2024–2025 routing ledger lists Ethics Topic 02 as the **first owner** of 2024 GS-IV Q2, with Topic 13 as co-owner for Q2(b). The stem is a **values-to-action ladder** — talk, belief, action — which is exactly this topic's translation problem applied to states rather than to officials.

**Demand decoding.** The stem supplies a three-rung ladder and an empirical premise, and then asks a single question: what are the *ethical considerations* of powerful nations. Two obligations follow. First, the ladder must be used, not merely quoted: an answer must show why belief without action is the failure the quotation names. Second, "ethical considerations" requires named principles applied to state conduct, not a survey of conflicts.

**Model answer (exam length — 147 words)**

The quotation sets a ladder: profession, conviction, action. Declaring peace costs nothing; believing it commits nothing; only acting on it surrenders an advantage. The ethical test for a powerful state is therefore whether it accepts a cost.

Four considerations follow. Non-maleficence limits arms transfers where diversion or use against civilians is foreseeable, not merely where it is proven. Consistency forbids condemning aggression selectively by alliance. Trusteeship of collective institutions requires that a permanent seat be exercised for the system rather than for the seat-holder. Transparency requires published end-use verification, since profit made invisible cannot be examined.

India's own position illustrates the standard: strategic autonomy, restraint in first use of force and support for negotiated settlement are commitments carrying real cost.

The limitation is candid. States pursue security and industrial employment, so ethical restraint must be built into enforceable export-control and verification regimes rather than left to declaration.

**Why this earns marks.** It uses the ladder analytically, names four specific ethical considerations rather than sentiments, supplies an India-centric anchor, and closes by converting the value into an institutional requirement instead of an appeal.

**Expansion bank (not part of the timed answer).** At greater length, add: Gandhi's means–ends coupling from ARC Box 2.8, since "commerce without morality" and "science without humanity" name this exact conduct; the distinction between an ethical duty and an enforceable treaty obligation; and the Kautilyan point that where an actor's gain is unobservable, disclosure regimes matter more than declarations.

**How to improve a weak attempt.** Replace any general condemnation of arms manufacturers with one named ethical principle plus the verification mechanism that would give it effect, and end on the trade-off between security interest and restraint rather than on a plea for peace.

---

#### Solved PYQ 2 — 2024 GS-IV, Q2(b) — 10 marks — 150 words — **directly owned**

**Question (verbatim official English wording):** Global warming and climate change are the outcomes of human greed in the name of development] indicating the direction in which extinction of organisms including human beings is heading towards loss of life on Earth. How do you put an end to this to protect life and bring equilibrium between the society and the environment.

**Source note:** exact English text from the locally held official PDF, question 2(b), page 2. The printed source carries a stray bracket after "development"; the artefact is preserved.

**Ownership note.** ✅ Routed to Ethics Topic 02 with Topic 13 as co-owner. This topic's contribution is the **value diagnosis** — the stem names *greed*, which is a value failure, and this topic owns aparigraha, trusteeship and the means–ends decoupling test. Topic 13 owns the environmental-ethics content in depth.

**Demand decoding.** The stem asserts a causal claim (greed produces climate change) and asks for a remedy delivering "equilibrium between the society and the environment". The directive is practical: **how**. An answer must therefore convert a value diagnosis into named instruments, and should test the stem's own premise rather than merely repeating it.

**Model answer (exam length — 141 words)**

The stem names greed, which is a value failure before it is a technical one: an end — development — pursued after its ethical means has been detached. Gandhi's warning against "commerce without morality" and Mahavir's aparigraha, non-possessiveness, both name the same fault.

Correction operates at three levels. Individually, sufficiency replaces accumulation: measured consumption, repair over replacement, and Mission LiFE-type behavioural commitments. Institutionally, prices must carry ecological cost through polluter-pays levies, honest environmental appraisal and public disclosure of emissions. Constitutionally, Article 51A(g) already obliges every citizen to protect and improve the natural environment and to have compassion for living creatures, while Article 21 jurisprudence protects a healthy environment.

The premise needs qualification. Poverty-driven resource use is need, not greed, and equity requires that restraint fall first on high-consumption groups.

Equilibrium therefore means bounded consumption enforced by pricing and disclosure, not development halted.

**Why this earns marks.** It accepts the value framing while refusing its overstatement, names an Indian constitutional anchor, supplies three levels of instrument, and distinguishes need from greed — the qualification that separates an examiner-grade answer from a moral essay.

**Expansion bank.** Add the intergenerational-equity principle; the common-but-differentiated-responsibilities framing for the international layer; and the observation that a household's option set, not its opinion, changes behaviour — the Savitribai Phule lesson applied to sustainability infrastructure.

**How to improve a weak attempt.** Replace generalised alarm with one named instrument per level and one qualification distinguishing subsistence use from surplus consumption.

---

#### Solved PYQ 3 — 2024 GS-IV, Q6(a) — 10 marks — 150 words — **directly owned**

**Question (verbatim official English wording):** "In Indian culture and value system, an equal opportunity has been provided irrespective of gender identity. The number of women in public service has been steadily increasing over the years." Examine the gender-specific challenges faced by female public servants and suggest suitable measures to increase their efficiency in discharging their duties and maintaining high standards of probity.

**Source note:** exact English text from the locally held official PDF, question 6(a), page 3.

**Ownership note.** ✅ The routing ledger lists Ethics Topic 02 as the **first owner** of 2024 GS-IV Q6, with Topic 04 as co-owner. The stem is a claim about the **Indian value system**, which is this topic's property; the foundational-values-for-civil-service dimension belongs to Topic 04.

**Demand decoding.** **Examine** requires a position with limits, and the stem opens with a proposition that should be tested rather than accepted. Two deliverables follow: gender-specific challenges, and measures serving *two* named ends — efficiency and probity. An answer that omits the probity limb answers half the question.

**Model answer (exam length — 149 words)**

Indian tradition contains egalitarian resources, and Article 51A(e) requires citizens to renounce practices derogatory to the dignity of women. Lived practice, however, has been unequal, which is why Savitribai Phule had to build India's first girls' school at Bhide Wada in 1848 rather than invoke an existing entitlement. The stem states an ideal, not a description.

Challenges are specific: unequal domestic burden constraining field postings and mobility; safety and infrastructure gaps in field housing and remote stations; credibility discounting in enforcement roles; harassment and its under-reporting; and interrupted career progression around caregiving.

Measures should be structural. Guarantee posting-linked housing, childcare and secure transport; make transfer policy transparent so caregiving is not penalised informally; enforce internal complaints committees with time-bound disposal; and count recorded reasoning, not only disposal volume, in appraisal — protecting probity by rewarding the officer who refuses improper pressure.

Equality requires enabling conditions, not merely declared opportunity.

**Why this earns marks.** It tests the stem's premise rather than repeating it, uses a dated Indian evidence anchor, separates challenges from measures, and satisfies the probity limb explicitly through the appraisal design point.

**Expansion bank.** Add graded inequality — women from historically excluded communities face compounded barriers, so disaggregation matters; the espoused-versus-enacted test applied to gender policy; and the hidden-curriculum analogue in departmental practice, where who is assigned which duty teaches more than any policy statement.

**How to improve a weak attempt.** Replace a general statement that "women face many challenges" with three specific, administratively addressable barriers, and ensure at least one measure is explicitly directed at probity rather than at welfare.

---

#### Solved PYQ 4 — 2024 GS-IV, Q6(b) — 10 marks — 150 words — **directly owned**

**Question (verbatim official English wording):** Mission Karmayogi is aiming for maintaining a very high standard of conduct and behaviour to ensure efficiency for serving citizens and in turn developing oneself. How will this scheme empower the civil servants in enhancing productive efficiency and delivering the services at the grassroots level.

**Source note:** exact English text from the locally held official PDF, question 6(b), page 4.

**Ownership note.** ✅ Routed to Ethics Topic 02 with Topic 04 as co-owner. Mission Karmayogi is this topic's current anchor precisely because it treats **value and behaviour formation in a serving adult** as a designed institutional process.

**Demand decoding.** The directive is **how**, so the answer must trace a mechanism from programme design to grassroots service, not describe the scheme. The stem itself names three outcomes — conduct and behaviour, efficiency for citizens, and self-development — and a complete answer touches all three and then bounds the claim.

**Model answer (exam length — 149 words)**

Mission Karmayogi, approved on 2 September 2020, shifts capacity building from rules to roles. Its FRAC framework specifies the competencies each role requires, and iGOT-Karmayogi delivers them continuously rather than once at induction; the Capacity Building Commission, established on 1 April 2021, supervises the design.

Empowerment operates through three channels. Role-specific competence reduces avoidable error at the counter, where citizens form their judgement of the administration. Behavioural competencies give officials a shared vocabulary for impartiality and citizen-centricity, making the standard nameable and therefore reviewable. Continuous, on-demand learning reaches frontline staff who were previously served only by episodic training; figures reported as on 6 February 2026 record 1.49 crore users, 4,342 courses, 7.26 crore completions and 23 languages.

The limit must be stated. These measure reach, not internalised conduct. Training holds only if appraisal counts the behaviour it teaches, and no general appraisal linkage should be assumed.

**Why this earns marks.** It gives dated institutional facts, converts them into three grassroots channels, and closes with the reinforcement-step limitation that distinguishes analysis from scheme description.

**Expansion bank.** Add ARC paragraph 2.7.2 on values not being easily imbibed after joining, which explains both the programme's necessity and its ceiling; the espoused-versus-enacted diagnostic; and the specific appraisal reform — grading the quality of recorded reasoning — that would close the gap.

**How to improve a weak attempt.** Replace a list of the scheme's features with one causal chain from competency specification to a named frontline improvement, and always attach a date to any figure cited.

---

#### Routed Demand 1 — 2022 GS-IV Q3(a) — 10 marks — neutral rendering — **directly owned**

**Neutral rendering of the routed demand:** Reflect on the idea that ethics consists in knowing the difference between what one has a right to do and what it is right to do.

**Source / ownership:** ⚠️ Neutral rendering from `_PYQ-ROUTING-MAINS-GS3-GS4-2018-2023.md`. The 2022 GS-IV paper is not held locally, so this is **not** presented as official wording. ✅ Routed to Ethics Topic 02 as its **only** listed owner. ⚠️ The line is widely attributed to Justice Potter Stewart, but no primary judicial opinion, speech or writing containing it has been traced.

**Demand decoding.** **Reflect** requires a considered position with at least one limitation. The core work is to establish that the legality–ethics gap is real, to show where it bites administratively, and to prevent the obvious over-correction into moral policing.

**Model answer (exam length — 149 words)**

The proposition distinguishes entitlement from rightness. Law fixes the outer boundary of permissible action; ethics asks what should be done inside it. Public office lives there, because discretion widens with seniority and no rulebook covers every situation.

The gap is documented officially. The Second ARC's Box 2.3 reproduces Senator Fulbright's question about those who "under the guise of friendship, accept favors, which offend the spirit of the law but do not violate its letter", and his warning that morality has become identical with legality.

Administratively, an officer may be entitled to accept hospitality during a live tender, to interpret a rule literally against a deserving claimant, or to withhold a discretionary sanction lawfully. Each is permissible and each damages impartiality.

The limit matters: the state may not punish conduct no rule prohibits. The correct response is preventive — declared thresholds, disclosure registers and recusal — not discipline for unproscribed acts.

**Why this earns marks.** It replaces an untraceable attribution with a documented official source, gives three concrete Indian administrative instances, and states the due-process limit that stops the principle collapsing into moral policing.

**Expansion bank.** Add the ARC's finding that conduct rules catalogue prohibited activities and "do not constitute a Code of Ethics"; the espoused-versus-enacted diagnostic; and the observation that the gap is exactly where the Commission recommends stipulating Public Service Values by law.

**How to improve a weak attempt.** Replace an abstract statement that legality differs from morality with one named permissible act that damages impartiality, and add the boundary that unproscribed conduct cannot be punished.

---

#### Routed Demand 2 — 2022 GS-IV Q3(b) — 10 marks — neutral rendering — **directly owned**

**Neutral rendering of the routed demand:** Reflect on a saying attributed to Dr A. P. J. Abdul Kalam on building a corruption-free nation and society.

**Source / ownership:** ⚠️ Neutral rendering from the 2018–2023 routing ledger; the 2022 paper is not held locally. ✅ Routed to Ethics Topic 02 as its only listed owner. ⚠️ Kalam spoke and wrote often on a corruption-free India, a theme echoed in *Indomitable Spirit* and in public addresses; treat the paper's precise wording as its own paraphrase unless independently verified.

**Demand decoding.** **Reflect** on a proposition that is generational and bottom-up. The answer must state the mechanism Kalam proposes, test it against the structural account of corruption, and reach a synthesis rather than endorsing or dismissing it.

**Model answer (exam length — 149 words)**

Kalam's proposition is bottom-up: a corruption-free nation is built by empowered youth, education and personal integrity multiplied across society, rather than by enforcement alone.

Its strength is that it addresses what enforcement cannot reach. The Second ARC concedes that rules "cannot provide for all situations" and that discretion widens with seniority, and it records at paragraph 2.7.2 that the relevant attitudes are "not skills which could be easily imbibed after joining the civil services". If formation happens before entry, education is not a supplement but the primary site.

Its limitation is equally clear. The same report locates corruption in opportunity and monopoly, and warns against an overemphasis on values and character that assumes nothing can be done until values are restored — a counsel of postponement.

The synthesis is therefore practical: pursue generational value formation while simultaneously shrinking discretionary monopoly, publishing criteria and disposing of disciplinary cases quickly.

**Why this earns marks.** It states the proposition's mechanism, supports it with the report's own timing finding, then supplies the report's own counter-warning, and closes with a synthesis rather than an endorsement.

**Expansion bank.** Add the Santhanam Committee's 1964 framing about idealism and patriotism in the ambition of youth, which shows the proposition has a sixty-year official pedigree in India; and the ARC's demand that disciplinary bodies "impose sanctions quickly", which is the enforcement half of the synthesis.

**How to improve a weak attempt.** Replace tribute with mechanism: name what education actually does to the value chain, then name the structural driver it cannot touch.

---

#### Routed Demand 3 — 2022 GS-IV Q3(c) — 10 marks — neutral rendering — **directly owned**

**Neutral rendering of the routed demand:** Reflect on a saying attributed to the Dalai Lama connecting success and sacrifice.

**Source / ownership:** ⚠️ Neutral rendering from the 2018–2023 routing ledger; the 2022 paper is not held locally. ✅ Routed to Ethics Topic 02 as its only listed owner. ⚠️ The most widely circulated matching line is "Judge your success by what you had to give up in order to get it"; treat it as widely attributed public teaching rather than a primary-sourced statement.

**Demand decoding.** **Reflect** on a proposition that redefines a measure. The examinable move is to convert it into an administrative performance question: what does the target-achievement figure conceal?

**Model answer (exam length — 148 words)**

The proposition redefines success as a net figure. An achievement is to be judged not only by what was gained but by what was surrendered to gain it. Applied to administration, it converts a target into a question about its price.

A district that reports full sanitation coverage by excluding hamlets from the denominator, or a police station that improves its disposal rate by refusing to register complaints, has achieved a number by surrendering truthfulness and equal access. The Second ARC's own insistence that ethical behaviour lies in "adoption in action" rather than in declared standards points the same way.

Operationally, this argues for measuring what was given up: exception registers, exclusion audits, complaint-refusal records and grievance data reported alongside achievement data.

The limitation is real. Every decision surrenders something, and outcomes still matter. The test is whether what was surrendered was a value or merely a preference.

**Why this earns marks.** It converts an inspirational line into a measurement design, supplies two concrete Indian instances, proposes named instruments, and prevents the principle from becoming an argument against targets altogether.

**Expansion bank.** Add the espoused-versus-enacted diagnostic; the Kautilyan point that unobservable conduct requires disclosure design rather than exhortation; and the observation that recording refusals of improper pressure is the positive counterpart of recording exclusions.

**How to improve a weak attempt.** Replace the paraphrase of the quotation with one named performance indicator that conceals a cost, and one register that would reveal it.

---

#### Routed Demand 4 — 2020 GS-IV Q5(a) — 10 marks — neutral rendering — **directly owned**

**Neutral rendering of the routed demand:** Discuss the main factors responsible for gender inequality in India, and the contribution of Savitribai Phule to its removal.

**Source / ownership:** ⚠️ Neutral rendering from the 2018–2023 routing ledger; the 2020 GS-IV paper is not held locally, so no verbatim claim is made. ✅ Routed to Ethics Topic 02 as first listed owner, with Topic 13 as co-owner for Q5(b).

**Demand decoding.** **Discuss** requires two linked deliverables in one answer: a causal account of gender inequality, and a contribution that acts on that account. The link is the whole test — a biography that does not connect to the named factors answers only half.

**Model answer (exam length — 149 words)**

Gender inequality in India is structural rather than attitudinal alone. Four factors reinforce one another: denial or truncation of girls' education; early marriage, which converts schooling into an apparent cost without return; caste-linked exclusion, which compounds barriers for women of historically excluded communities; and unpaid care work, which limits mobility and public participation. Each makes the others rational from a household's point of view.

Savitribai Phule's contribution acts precisely on that system. With Jyotirao Phule she co-founded India's first girls' school at Bhide Wada, Pune, in 1848, faced documented social hostility, and later supported widow remarriage and opposed child marriage and caste discrimination. She did not principally exhort; she supplied the missing institution and thereby changed the household's option set.

The method now has constitutional form in Article 21A and Article 51A(k). Its limitation is honest: one institution is not a system, and correction required sustained, generation-spanning reform.

**Why this earns marks.** It names four interlocking factors rather than listing grievances, shows the contribution acting on the named system, converts the historical act into a present constitutional instrument, and concedes a limitation.

**Expansion bank.** Add the graded-inequality point requiring disaggregation of "women" as a category; the hidden-curriculum layer, where mid-day-meal practice teaches more than the syllabus; and the ARC 2.7.2 generational horizon.

**How to improve a weak attempt.** Replace a tribute paragraph with the causal link — name the factor, then name the institution built against it, then state what one institution could not achieve.

---

#### Routed Demand 5 — 2020 GS-IV Q1(b) — 10 marks — neutral rendering — **directly owned (education limb)**

**Neutral rendering of the routed demand:** Discuss education as a pervasive tool for individual development and social transformation, with reference to the National Education Policy 2020.

**Source / ownership:** ⚠️ Neutral rendering from the 2018–2023 routing ledger; the 2020 paper is not held locally. ✅ Routed to Ethics Topic 01 and Topic 02. Q1(a) on Comprehensive National Power belongs primarily to Topic 01; **this education limb is Topic 02's**, because "role of educational institutions in inculcating values" is the printed clause this topic owns.

**Demand decoding.** **Discuss** requires both halves — individual development *and* social transformation — with the policy named. The value dimension is what makes this a GS-IV rather than a GS-II answer.

**Model answer (exam length — 149 words)**

Education is pervasive because it forms capability and disposition together. Individually, it develops competence, reasoning and the confidence to claim entitlements; Article 51A(h) names the target as scientific temper, humanism and the spirit of inquiry and reform.

Socially, it transforms in two ways. It alters option sets, which is why building a school changes behaviour more reliably than exhortation does. And it transmits values through practice: seating, task allocation and whether the mid-day meal is eaten together teach equality or hierarchy more powerfully than any chapter. NEP 2020 is the current policy vehicle for that intent, with its emphasis on foundational learning, flexibility and value orientation.

The Second ARC supplies both the case and the caution: such attitudes are "not skills which could be easily imbibed after joining the civil services", yet the right ethos "takes long to evolve" across successive generations.

Article 21A makes the entitlement justiciable.

**Why this earns marks.** It covers both halves, distinguishes the option-set channel from the value-transmission channel, cites the constitutional instruments with their correct status, and bounds the claim with a generational horizon.

**Expansion bank.** Add the Santhanam Committee's 1964 statement; the distinction between the justiciable Article 21A and the non-justiciable Article 51A(k); and the caution that schools reproduce inequality where enrolment and attention track caste, gender and income.

**How to improve a weak attempt.** Replace praise of education with one named mechanism per half, and add the caution that a school can transmit hierarchy as effectively as equality.

---

#### Cross-topic anchor 1 — 2024 GS-IV, Q3(a) — 10 marks — 150 words

**Question (verbatim official English wording):** Given below are three quotations of great thinkers. What do each of these quotations convey to you in the present context. (a) "Learn everything that is good from others, but bring it in, and in your own way absorb it, do not become others." -Swami Vivekananda

**Source note:** exact English text from the locally held official PDF, question 3(a), pages 2–3.

**Ownership note.** ⚠️ The 2024–2025 routing ledger assigns 2024 GS-IV Q3 to **Ethics Topics 06 and 07**, not to Topic 02. It is retained here as a cross-topic anchor because the borrowing principle is this topic's Session 12 content and because the canonical Basic owner teaches it. It is **not** counted in the owned total.

**Model answer (exam length — 147 words)**

The line separates learning from imitation. A society or an administration should absorb what is good on its own terms rather than reproduce a foreign form whole.

Applied to reform, it yields a working test: identify the mechanism that produced the result elsewhere, preserve that mechanism, and redesign everything downstream for Indian conditions. If a single-window grievance system works because of a statutory disposal deadline with automatic escalation, that deadline must survive the transfer; assisted counters, local-language interfaces and offline receipts are legitimate adaptations for low-connectivity blocks.

The principle also cuts against itself, which is worth one line. "Adaptation" is the standard vocabulary in which a reform's substance is negotiated away while its label is kept. The honest test is whether the causal mechanism survived, not whether the local variant feels comfortable.

Vivekananda's instruction is therefore ethical self-respect joined to openness — informed adaptation, neither transplant nor refusal.

**Why this earns marks.** It converts the quotation into a decision procedure, supplies a concrete Indian illustration, and states the principle's own misuse — the qualification most answers omit.

**Provenance.** ✅ Traceable to the *Complete Works*, Volume III, "The Common Bases of Hinduism", Lahore, 1897 — one of the few lines in this area with an identified primary location.

---

#### Cross-topic anchor 2 — 2024 GS-IV, Q3(b) — 10 marks — 150 words

**Question (verbatim official English wording):** Anslotte sarfre "Faith is of no avail in the absence of strength. Faith and strength, both are essential to accomplish any great work-" -Sardar Patel

**Source note:** exact English text from the locally held official PDF, question 3(b), page 3. The printed source carries the stray characters `Anslotte sarfre` before the quotation; the artefact is reproduced rather than silently removed.

**Ownership note.** ⚠️ Routed to Topics 06 and 07. Cross-topic anchor; not counted as owned. ⚠️ No primary Patel speech or letter has been traced for the line; it is the paper's attribution.

**Model answer (exam length — 150 words)**

The proposition is that conviction and capacity are multiplicative, not additive. Faith supplies direction and legitimacy; strength — competence, organisation, courage and persistence — converts direction into result. Either alone fails: a values declaration without a capability plan is a press note, and capability without a value is force looking for a purpose.

Administration illustrates the pairing. Belief in disaster preparedness is worthless without trained teams, tested warning chains, accessible shelters and transparent relief lists; conversely, a well-resourced enforcement machinery without ethical purpose becomes coercive and serves the influential. A District Magistrate committed to girls' education must also secure transport, safe premises and teacher deployment, or the commitment produces nothing.

One caution belongs in a clause: the line is set by the paper as Sardar Patel's without a traced primary source, which does not weaken the idea.

Public service therefore needs values to choose the end and capability to reach it lawfully.

**Why this earns marks.** It reads the relationship as multiplicative rather than as a list, gives a two-sided administrative illustration, includes the attribution caution in a single clause, and closes on a bounded verdict.

---

#### Cross-topic anchor 3 — 2025 GS-IV, Q3(a) — 10 marks — 150 words

**Question (verbatim official English wording):** "Those who in trouble untroubled are, Will trouble trouble itself." – Thiruvalluvar

**Source note:** exact English text from the locally held official PDF `books\mains\UPSC Mains 2025 GS Paper 4.pdf`, question 3(a), page 2.

**Ownership note.** ⚠️ Routed to Topics 06 and 07. Cross-topic anchor; not counted as owned. ⚠️ The wording is a modern paraphrase rather than a named historic translation of **Tirukkural 623** (Chapter 63, *Iṭukkaṇ Aḻiyāmai*); G. U. Pope renders it "Who griefs confront with meek, ungrieving heart, / From them griefs, put to grief, depart."

**Model answer (exam length — 149 words)**

The couplet makes composure an active force. A calm mind does not deny danger; it prevents fear and confusion from multiplying the original problem. For a civil servant, equanimity is therefore operational rather than decorative, because accurate assessment and humane communication are hardest exactly when they matter most.

In a cloudburst or a communal rumour, an officer who stays composed verifies facts before announcing them, activates response teams in sequence, states clearly what is known and unknown, protects vulnerable groups first, and records decisions for later scrutiny. Panic spreads misinformation and invites arbitrary action; disciplined calm allows police, health workers and local representatives to be coordinated rather than merely instructed.

Equanimity has a boundary. It is not passivity, emotional coldness, or delay disguised as patience. An officer who remains serene while failing to order rescue or hear grievances has inverted the teaching. Composure must accompany urgency, not replace it.

**Why this earns marks.** It treats composure as a mechanism with named crisis behaviours, and it supplies the limitation — equanimity is not passivity — that the quotation itself does not contain.

---

#### Cross-topic anchor 4 — 2025 GS-IV, Q3(b) — 10 marks — 150 words

**Question (verbatim official English wording):** "The greatest discovery of my generation is that a human being can alter his life by altering his attitudes." – William James

**Source note:** exact English text from the locally held official PDF, question 3(b), page 2. ⚠️ The Hindi text on the same page renders the name as "William Jones"; the English printing is the operative one and the discrepancy is recorded as an observed feature of the paper.

**Ownership note.** ⚠️ Routed to Topics 06 and 07. Cross-topic anchor; not counted as owned. ⚠️ The line is widely attributed to James but is not traceable to his writings and was popularised by Norman Vincent Peale in 1952; a genuine James statement of the theme, from 1895, is "Believe that life is worth living, and your belief will help create the fact."

**Model answer (exam length — 150 words)**

The claim is that disposition is authored rather than inherited: what a person can do is bounded partly by how the situation is construed, and construal can be changed by deliberate effort.

For public administration this has three consequences. It makes adult training worthwhile, since attitudes are malleable and behavioural competence can be built after entry. It locates a lever in reframing: an officer who treats a grievance as information rather than as an accusation will investigate rather than defend. And it supports reflective practice — structured debriefing after real decisions — as a method of revising working assumptions.

The limitation must be explicit. Attitude change cannot dissolve structural constraint. A frontline worker cannot resolve staff shortage, unpaid dues or an unsafe posting by thinking differently about them, and telling her to do so converts a systemic failure into a personal deficiency.

Attitudes are therefore a real lever, and a bounded one.

**Why this earns marks.** It names three administrative consequences rather than restating the quotation, and it supplies the structural-constraint limitation that prevents the answer from becoming motivational advice.

---

#### Cross-topic anchor 5 — 2025 GS-IV, Q3(c) — 10 marks — 150 words

**Question (verbatim official English wording):** "The strength of a society is not in its laws, but in the morality of its people." – Swami Vivekananda

**Source note:** exact English text from the locally held official PDF, question 3(c), page 3.

**Ownership note.** ⚠️ Routed to Topics 06 and 07. Cross-topic anchor; not counted as owned. ⚠️ The line is set by UPSC as Vivekananda's but is **not traceable** to the *Complete Works*. The nearest documented statement of the theme, from "Our Duty to the Masses" (23 June 1894), is: "No country on earth has so many laws, and in no country are they so little regarded."

**Model answer (exam length — 149 words)**

The proposition holds that law is necessary but not sufficient: a statute is enforced by people, interpreted by people and obeyed largely without supervision, so its practical strength depends on the standards its society has internalised.

The claim has real administrative force. The Second ARC concedes that rules "cannot provide for all situations" and that discretion widens with seniority, and its Preface states that "one cannot mandate honesty". Where a value is absent, enforcement must observe everything — which is impossible and expensive.

But the proposition, taken strictly, understates the same report's diagnosis. It locates official corruption in opportunity, monopoly and unobservability rather than in popular morality, and it warns against an overemphasis on values that assumes nothing can be done until values are restored.

The defensible synthesis is reciprocal: law without internalised morality is under-enforced because enforcement itself needs honest enforcers, while morality without law is unenforceable at scale.

**Why this earns marks.** It accepts the proposition's force, then tests it against the primary report's own structural diagnosis, and closes with a reciprocal synthesis rather than an endorsement.

---

#### Cross-topic anchor 6 — 2025 GS-IV, Q4(b) — 10 marks — 150 words

**Question (verbatim official English wording):** What are the major teachings of Mahavir? Explain their relevance in the contemporary world.

**Source note:** exact English text from the locally held official PDF, question 4(b), page 3.

**Ownership note.** ⚠️ Routed to Topics 04 and 06. Cross-topic anchor; not counted as owned. Doctrinal depth belongs to Topic 06 and to the Philosophy Jainism file; this topic's contribution is the value-to-administration translation.

**Model answer (exam length — 144 words)**

Mahavir's teachings comprise five great vows and two doctrines about truth. The vows are ahimsa, non-injury; satya, truthfulness; asteya, non-stealing; brahmacharya, restraint; and aparigraha, non-possessiveness. The doctrines are anekantavada, the many-sidedness of reality, and syadvada, conditional predication, which prefixes an assertion with *syat*, "from a standpoint".

Their contemporary relevance is specific. Ahimsa supplies a proportionality test in enforcement and eviction. Satya requires accurate reporting rather than favourable figures. Aparigraha is the ethical core of probity, since office is held in trust and not owned, which is why conflict-of-interest and anti-nepotism rules exist. Anekantavada makes multi-stakeholder consultation an information requirement rather than a formality. Syadvada disciplines official note-writing: state the standpoint from which a finding holds.

One precision point and one caution. Jain tradition contrasts Parshvanatha's four restraints with Mahavir's five. And anekantavada is epistemic humility, not moral relativism or a licence to defer decisions.

**Why this earns marks.** It answers both halves, translates each teaching into a named administrative practice rather than a sentiment, and closes with the precision point and the standard trap.

---

#### Cross-topic anchor 7 — 2019 GS-IV Q6(c) — 10 marks — neutral rendering

**Neutral rendering of the routed demand:** Reflect on the saying that where there is righteousness in the heart there is beauty in the character, where there is beauty in the character there is harmony in the home, where there is harmony in the home there is order in the nation, and where there is order in the nation there is peace in the world.

**Source / ownership:** ⚠️ Neutral rendering from the 2018–2023 routing ledger; the 2019 paper is not held locally. ⚠️ Routed to **Topics 06 and 07**, so it is a cross-topic anchor and is not counted as owned, notwithstanding that the canonical Basic owner discusses it here. ⚠️ Dr Kalam popularised the chain — for example in his April 2007 address to the European Parliament — but explicitly credited it there to Sathya Sai Baba.

**Model answer (exam length — 147 words)**

The saying proposes a bottom-up theory of order: personal integrity forms character, character shapes the household, households make a stable polity, and stable polities make peace. Its administrative value is that it refuses the separation of private ethics from public consequence.

The same shape appears in official Indian reform literature. The Second ARC's Preface quotes Confucius that a ruler who governs by his virtues "may be compared to the Pole star which keeps its place and all other stars turn towards it", and adds that when the ruler is right the people follow, while derelict governance makes the governed suffer. Leadership by visible example is therefore a documented governance proposition, not merely an inspirational one.

Its limitation must be stated plainly. The chain is rhetorical rather than demonstrated: national order also depends on institutions, law and material capacity, and integrity alone has never produced peace between states.

**Why this earns marks.** It states the mechanism, corroborates the shape from a primary official source rather than resting on the attribution, and explicitly labels the chain as rhetorical rather than causal.

---

### ORIGINAL MAINS PRACTICE WITH MODEL SOLUTIONS

**Format note.** ✅ The 10-mark items below follow the verified GS-IV Section-A format (150 words). ✅ The 20-mark items follow the verified GS-IV Section-B format, which is a **case study** (250 words). ⚠️ The 15-mark items are **extended analytical practice and are not a GS-IV paper format**; they are retained to satisfy the repository's 10/15/20 requirement and to build depth.

#### Original Mains Practice 1 — 10 marks — 150 words

**Question:** "Values are inculcated, not instructed." Explain the mechanisms through which family, society and educational institutions actually transmit values, and identify the failure mode of each. (Answer in 150 words)

**Model answer (146 words)**

Transmission works through five mechanisms, of which explicit instruction is the weakest alone. Modelling transmits by observed conduct: a child copies how a scarce resource is divided at home, and a junior copies what a senior actually does. Reinforcement transmits by what is rewarded. Identification transmits through belonging — the Second ARC notes that where office brings prestige, dismissal "means disgrace for the entire family", giving relatives an incentive to restrain the officer. Instruction transmits content. Reflective revision transmits by testing inherited standards against experience.

Each has a failure mode. The family models acquisitiveness and produces nepotism. Society transmits resignation; the ARC records corruption being regarded as "inevitable" and resistance as "futile". Schools reproduce hierarchy through the hidden curriculum whatever the syllabus prints. Instruction fails when taught as recall.

Diagnosis therefore precedes remedy: name the failing mechanism, because a modelling failure is not cured by a curriculum.

**Why this earns marks.** It names five mechanisms with a primary-source anchor for the strongest, attaches a specific failure mode to each institution, and closes with the diagnostic rule rather than a general appeal.

---

#### Original Mains Practice 2 — 10 marks — 150 words

**Question:** Examine the proposition that the Fundamental Duties in Article 51A are an effective instrument for inculcating values in Indian citizens. (Answer in 150 words)

**Model answer (147 words)**

Article 51A, inserted by the 42nd Amendment in 1976 on the Swaran Singh Committee's recommendation and extended to eleven duties by the 86th Amendment in 2002, is a genuine value instrument in one respect and a weak one in another.

Its strength is declaratory and educative. It converts contested standards into a public text: 51A(b) requires citizens to cherish the ideals of the freedom struggle, 51A(e) to renounce practices derogatory to the dignity of women, and 51A(h) to develop scientific temper. Indira Gandhi's stated justification was to make people conscious of duties as they are of rights.

Its weakness is enforceability. The duties are non-justiciable, carry no legal sanction, and Parliament expressly declined the Committee's proposals for a penalty and for immunity of such a penalty law from challenge.

The verdict is therefore bounded: effective as declared standard and interpretive aid, ineffective as command.

**Why this earns marks.** It supplies exact amendment history, cites three duties with their content, uses the rejected penalty clause as proof of design intent, and reaches a bounded verdict on both limbs.

---

#### Original Mains Practice 3 — 10 marks — 150 words

**Question:** "A quotation is worth writing in a GS-IV answer only when it changes what an officer is required to do." Justify this statement with suitable examples. (Answer in 150 words)

**Model answer (143 words)**

The statement is justified because the examinable skill is translation, not recall. A teaching that leaves an officer's obligations unchanged has added no information to the answer.

Two examples show the difference. Gandhi's "commerce without morality" changes an obligation: it directs a procurement officer facing under-invoicing pressure to record the pressure on file and refer the deviation to the tender committee, converting restraint into an auditable act. Kautilya's five propositions, as the Second ARC quotes them, change a design obligation: disgorgement and reassignment for proven dishonesty, but published minimum tenure as a reward for integrity.

By contrast, an answer that merely asserts that great leaders valued honesty leaves every procedure exactly where it was.

The qualification is that translation must be honest. Where a quotation's provenance is untraceable, the idea should be answered with a clause of caution, never with an invented citation.

**Why this earns marks.** It defends the proposition with two contrasting worked examples, names the resulting obligations precisely, and closes with the attribution discipline that the practice itself requires.

---

#### Original Mains Practice 4 — 15 marks — extended analytical practice (not a GS-IV paper format) — 250 words

**Question:** Compare Gandhi's character-centred account of ethical failure with Kautilya's opportunity-centred account, as both are recorded in the Second ARC's Fourth Report. What balanced integrity design follows for a district administration? (Answer in about 250 words)

**Model answer (250 words)**

The Second ARC records both registers and uses both. Gandhi's, at Box 2.8, presents the Seven Social Sins — politics without principles through to worship without sacrifice — one fault repeated seven times: a legitimate end pursued after its ethical means has been detached. Kautilya's, in Annexure-I(2), presents five propositions: custody makes tasting inevitable, appropriation is as unobservable as a fish drinking water, intent is more concealed than the path of birds, wrongful gains must be disgorged and the officer changed in his works, and those who increase wealth in just ways must be made permanent in office.

The comparison is not of quality but of domain. Gandhi addresses conduct where observation cannot reach — the residue the Commission concedes at paragraph 2.2.4 when it says rules "cannot provide for all situations" and that discretion widens with seniority. Kautilya addresses the observable structure that determines how large that residue is.

Each fails alone. Character-first reasoning drifts into the "overemphasis on values and character" the Commission warns against at paragraph 1.4, whose assumption is that nothing can be done until values are restored. Design-first reasoning drifts into surveillance that demoralises honest officers and induces decision-avoidance.

A balanced district design follows. Shrink the residue: publish eligibility criteria, auto-allot files, timestamp stages, and end single-officer monopoly on discretionary decisions. Fill it: require recorded reasons for every deviation, and grade their quality in appraisal. Reward integrity through published minimum-tenure norms, and make one senior recusal visible each year, since modelling transmits fastest.

**Why this earns marks.** It reproduces both accounts accurately from the primary report, resolves the comparison by domain rather than by preference, names each register's failure mode, and converts the synthesis into four concrete district-level instruments.

---

#### Original Mains Practice 5 — 15 marks — extended analytical practice (not a GS-IV paper format) — 250 words

**Question:** Critically examine the claim that exemplar-based ethics — teaching values through the lives of great leaders — is an unreliable foundation for public administration. (Answer in about 250 words)

**Model answer (249 words)**

The claim has real force. Exemplar reasoning fails in six identifiable ways. It becomes a quotation scrapbook, in which sayings replace rules. It permits selective quotation, since a substantial corpus usually supports opposite administrative choices. It invites anachronism, as when Kautilya is credited with designing a vigilance commission rather than diagnosing an incentive problem. It slides into hero-worship, treating admired character as a warrant for any inferred position. It enables moral substitution — the Second ARC itself warns at paragraph 1.4 against an overemphasis on values and character whose implicit assumption is that "until values are restored, nothing much can be done", a counsel of postponement. And it tempts fabricated provenance, since several lines UPSC has set, including those attributed to Sardar Patel, William James and Justice Potter Stewart, cannot be traced to a primary source.

But the claim overstates. Exemplars do work that neither codes nor sanctions can do. They make a standard memorable under time pressure. They make it credible, by demonstrating that a real person kept it under pressure — something no notification can establish. And they extend moral imagination beyond the current rulebook. The Constitution recognises this at Article 51A(b), which makes cherishing the ideals of the freedom struggle a citizen duty.

The reasoned verdict is bounded rather than dismissive. Exemplars are inputs to conduct, not guarantees of it. They earn their place when each is carried through to a named administrative rule with a stated limitation, and lose it the moment admiration replaces mechanism.

**Why this earns marks.** It takes the critical side seriously with six named failure modes and a primary-source warning, then supplies the counter-case with a constitutional anchor, and ends on a bounded verdict rather than on either dismissal or celebration.

---

#### Original Mains Practice 6 — 20 marks — GS-IV Section-B case study — 250 words

**Case:** You are the Deputy Commissioner of a district. A large private school, run by a trust whose chairperson is a politically influential person, has been sanctioned land at a concessional rate on condition that it reserves a quarter of its seats for children from disadvantaged families. An inspection you ordered finds that these children are seated in a separate section, taught by the least experienced staff, and required to eat the mid-day meal after the fee-paying children have finished. The trust's records are formally compliant: the quota is filled and no rule expressly prohibits sectioning. The chairperson tells you, politely, that any adverse finding will be challenged and that your predecessor "understood the practical difficulties". A parents' group has approached you but is afraid of losing seats. The district education officer advises that a show-cause notice would be legally fragile.

**(a)** Identify the stakeholders and the values in conflict. **(b)** Evaluate at least three options. **(c)** State your decision, its implementation, and the residual risk. (Answer in 250 words)

**Model answer (248 words)**

**Stakeholders and values.** The quota children and their parents hold claims to equal dignity and non-discrimination; fee-paying parents hold an interest in continuity; the trust holds a legitimate expectation of lawful process; the teachers are subject to allocation decisions; and the public holds an interest in enforcement of a concession condition. The conflict is between formal compliance and substantive equality, and between avoiding legal fragility and refusing to normalise segregation.

**Options.** First, accept the record. It is safe and wrong: it treats the quota as a headcount and permits the hidden curriculum to teach hierarchy daily, which is precisely how a school transmits values. Second, issue an immediate show-cause notice. It is principled but, as advised, legally fragile on the sectioning point, and it exposes the complaining parents. Third, act on the enforceable instrument. The concession was granted on a condition; conditions are enforceable on their own terms, and the meal and staffing practices are documentable facts.

**Decision.** Adopt the third option, with the first two elements of the second. Record the inspection findings with photographs and timings. Issue a notice under the land-concession condition, not under a general moral standard. Direct integrated sections and a common meal sitting from the next term, with staffing allocation returned by lot. Protect the parents by acting on the inspection record rather than on their complaint.

**Residual risk.** Litigation, and pressure on my posting. Mitigation: a speaking order, a copy to the sanctioning department, and a compliance review in ninety days.

**Why this earns marks.** It names stakeholders and values precisely, evaluates three genuinely distinct options including the tempting safe one, selects the enforceable instrument rather than the moral one, protects the vulnerable complainant, and states an honest residual risk with mitigation.

---

#### Original Mains Practice 7 — 20 marks — GS-IV Section-B case study — 250 words

**Case:** You head a training academy for a state civil service. Your predecessor introduced a well-regarded ethics module, and completion rates are near total. Yet a confidential survey of officers three years after training reports that recorded refusals of improper pressure are almost non-existent, that the appraisal counts only disposal volume, and that two of the academy's own senior faculty have relatives who regularly clear examinations they help set. A junior faculty member brings you this survey and asks that it be suppressed, fearing that publishing it will end the module's funding. The state government has asked you for a note on "strengthening value education" ahead of a budget decision.

**(a)** What are the ethical issues? **(b)** What options are available and what would each achieve? **(c)** What will you do, and what safeguards will you build? (Answer in 250 words)

**Model answer (249 words)**

**Ethical issues.** Three, and they are distinct. First, an integrity issue inside the academy: faculty relatives clearing examinations the faculty help set is a conflict of interest regardless of outcome. Second, an honesty issue about the survey: suppressing inconvenient evidence to protect a programme is the reasoning the programme exists to prevent. Third, a design issue: near-total completion alongside near-zero recorded refusals shows a value taught at the formation step and contradicted at the reinforcement step.

**Options.** Suppress and seek funding. It protects the module and destroys the academy's standing to teach integrity; it also models concealment to every officer who later learns of it. Publish and blame the service. It is honest but shifts responsibility away from the institution that trained them. Publish with a remedy attached. It uses the survey as the evidence base for the note the government has asked for.

**Decision.** Take the third. Send the survey to government as an annexure to the note. Recommend one appraisal change — grade the quality of recorded reasoning in discretionary decisions, audited on a sample — because conduct is repeated when it is rewarded, and no training budget defeats an appraisal form. Simultaneously order faculty recusal from any examination involving a relative, publish the recusal register, and record it as the academy's first application of the module.

**Safeguards and residual risk.** Protect the junior faculty member by attributing the survey institutionally. Risk: funding may still be cut. Mitigation: present the recusal reform as evidence that the academy corrects itself.

**Why this earns marks.** It separates three genuinely different ethical issues, refuses the tempting protective option with a stated reason, converts the diagnosis into one high-leverage institutional reform, protects the person who raised the matter, and concedes the residual risk with a mitigation.

---

## OPTIONAL ADVANCED DEPTH — NOT REQUIRED FOR A CORE ANSWER

This block reproduces and reorganises the canonical Advanced owner. Nothing here is needed to write a complete Basic answer; it refines, extends and bounds what has already been taught.

### A1. The architecture in one frame

```text
VALUE FORMATION                 VALUE TESTING                 VALUE APPLICATION
family/education/culture   ->   discretion + pressure    ->   translated lesson from an exemplar
(source, largely fixed          (institutional/political      (Gandhi/Vivekananda/Kautilya/
 by early adulthood)             environment, ARC Ch.1)        Mahavir-type reasoning)
                                        |
                                        v
                     EXAMINER TESTS: can the candidate move from
                     quotation -> present-context administrative lesson
                     without collapsing into biography or moral cliche?
```

⚠️ **Analytical claim.** GS-IV's leader and quotation questions are not history questions; they are **applied-ethics translation tests**. The examiner rewards a candidate who can derive a specific, defensible administrative rule from a general moral teaching and then bound that rule with a realistic limitation.

### A2. Two advanced concepts and distinctions

| Concept | Precise meaning |
|---|---|
| ⚠️ **Value internalisation vs value performance** | Internalisation means the value genuinely shapes private choice even when unobserved; performance means the value is displayed only for external audit or appraisal. The ethical-dilemma literature treats only the former as robust under pressure. |
| ✅ **Nishkama karma** (Gita) | Duty performed without attachment to result — repeatedly tested through "devotion to duty" quotations, including the 2025 GS-IV Q5(a) demand. ⚠️ It means non-attachment to personal credit or reward for the outcome, **not** indifference to whether the public duty is performed well. |
| ✅ **Anekantavada / Syadvada** (Mahavir) | The many-sidedness of truth and conditional predication, supporting epistemic humility in policy-making rather than relativism about right and wrong. |
| ⚠️ **Exemplar fallacy** | The risk of citing a leader's life uncritically as if perfect virtue automatically resolved a complex institutional dilemma. Advanced answers must show *where* the exemplar's principle has limits. |

### A3. Gandhi — the deeper reading

- ✅ Reproduced in ARC **Box 2.8** and sourced there to Gandhi in *Young India*, 1925 — a prescient warning against decoupling ends (wealth, pleasure, knowledge, politics, commerce, worship) from ethical means (work, conscience, humanity, character, principle, morality, sacrifice).
- ⚠️ Provenance nuance worth one line in an answer: in the *Young India* note of 22 October 1925 Gandhi presented the list as one his correspondent wanted readers to know; the ARC's Box also prints "leisure without conscience" where the original reads "pleasure without conscience". Cite it as a list Gandhi published and endorsed.
- ⚠️ Administrative reading: each "sin" names a **means–ends decoupling**. "Politics without principles" anticipates the Commission's own later diagnosis that corrupt political markets displace rather than eliminate corruption.
- ⚠️ Limitation: as a list of prohibitions it supplies no enforcement mechanism. It must be paired with institutional design — Nolan-type codes, treated in Topics 09 and 16 — consistent with the values-plus-institutions synthesis of Topic 01.

### A4. Vivekananda — the two tested quotations, synthesised

- ✅ "Learn everything that is good from others, but bring it in, and in your own way absorb it; do not become others" (2024) is traceable to the *Complete Works*, Vol. III, "The Common Bases of Hinduism" (Lahore, 1897).
- ⚠️ "The strength of a society is not in its laws, but in the morality of its people" (2025) is set by UPSC as Vivekananda's but is **not traceable** to the *Complete Works*. The nearest documented statement of the theme is from "Our Duty to the Masses" (23 June 1894): "No country on earth has so many laws, and in no country are they so little regarded." Answer the proposition; do not manufacture a citation.
- ⚠️ Read together they still form a coherent position: **selective institutional borrowing** plus the **primacy of internalised societal morality over external law**.
- ⚠️ Advanced application: when India adapts a foreign anti-corruption model — Hong Kong's ICAC or Singapore's CPIB, treated in Topic 23 — Vivekananda's principle argues for adaptation to Indian federal, political and cultural conditions rather than transplant. This is directly relevant to why the Lokpal took decades longer to operationalise than the Commission's 2007 recommendation envisaged.

### A5. Kautilya — the earliest structural theory, in full

- ✅ Quoted in ARC **Annexure-I(2)**, the National Colloquium speech of Chairman M. Veerappa Moily dated 1 September 2006, which reproduces five *Arthasastra* propositions: officials handling the king's money can no more avoid tasting it than a tongue can avoid tasting honey or poison (Kangle 2.9.32); embezzlement is as unobservable as a fish drinking water (2.9.33); "it is possible to know even the path of birds flying in the sky, but not the ways of officers moving with their intentions concealed" (2.9.34); those who have amassed money wrongfully should be made to disgorge it and be moved to other work; and those who increase the king's wealth "in just ways" should be made permanent in their offices.
- ⚠️ This is a **structural or opportunity theory** of corruption predating modern institutional economics by roughly two millennia. The prescribed remedy — positive permanence for the honest, disgorgement and reassignment for the rest — anticipates today's performance-linked, rotation-based vigilance mechanisms treated in Topic 21.
- ⚠️ Advanced distinction: Kautilya's realism (assume temptation is near-universal and design controls accordingly) sits in productive tension with Gandhi's character-based idealism. A strong Mains answer can use *both* registers depending on the question's framing — systemic design against personal conduct.

### A6. Mahavir — anekantavada as an administrative epistemology

- ✅ The 2025 GS-IV Q4(b) demand tested "major teachings of Mahavir" and their "relevance in the contemporary world" — the five *Mahavratas* of ahimsa, satya, asteya, brahmacharya and aparigraha, plus anekantavada and syadvada.
- ⚠️ Precision point worth one line: Jain tradition contrasts Parshvanatha's four restraints with Mahavir's five. Brahmacharya is best described as the vow Mahavir's tradition *separately enumerated*, having earlier been understood as included within aparigraha — not as an ethical idea Mahavir is documented to have invented.
- ⚠️ Administrative translation: anekantavada supports structured multi-stakeholder consultation — before a land-acquisition or environmental-clearance decision — as an epistemic discipline rather than procedural box-ticking. It says complex public decisions are irreducibly many-sided.
- ⚠️ Aparigraha maps onto conflict-of-interest and anti-nepotism norms in public office. An official's non-attachment to personal gain from office is the ethical core of probity, treated in full in Topic 14.

### A7. Institutional translation of value education

- 📰 Mission Karmayogi / NPCSCB, approved by the Union Cabinet on **2 September 2020**, builds behavioural competencies — values, attitude, integrity — into training through the **FRAC** approach and the **iGOT-Karmayogi** platform, with the **Capacity Building Commission** established on **1 April 2021**. Official scale, as reported to Parliament with data as on **6 February 2026**: over 1.49 crore users, 4,342 courses, 7.26 crore course completions and 23 languages. Do not assert a general APAR or appraisal linkage without a service-specific DoPT order.
- ⚠️ Value education in Mains answers should therefore reference *both* the classical exemplar and the current institutional delivery mechanism, showing the values-plus-institutions synthesis in practice.

### A8. Indian applications and boundary cases

- ⚠️ A civil servant applying Vivekananda's selective-borrowing principle while localising a Bhoomi or Gyandoot-style e-governance model (Topic 23) for a state with weaker digital literacy — the limitation being that "adaptation" must not become an excuse to dilute the reform's substance.
- ⚠️ A civil servant applying Kautilya's rotation-and-reward logic through a transparent, rule-bound transfer policy (Topic 21) rather than ad hoc reassignment — the boundary being that rotation itself can be misused as a punitive tool if not rule-bound.

### A9. Limitations and trade-offs

- ⚠️ Exemplar-based reasoning risks selective quotation: a leader's teaching can be invoked to justify opposite administrative choices unless the specific institutional context is stated.
- ⚠️ Kautilya's assume-everyone-is-tempted realism, if taken alone, can justify excessive and demoralising surveillance of honest officials. Topic 21 records the Commission's explicit caution against that over-correction.
- ⚠️ Gandhi's character-first idealism, if taken alone, under-weights the Commission's own institutional diagnosis that discretion and monopoly — not character alone — drive corruption.

### A10. Advanced factual anchors

- ✅ The "Seven Social Sins" list appears in the Fourth Report as **Box 2.8**, sourced there to Gandhi in *Young India*, 1925 (original note dated 22 October 1925, Collected Works Vol. XXVIII).
- ✅ Mahavir's five *Mahavratas*: ahimsa, satya, asteya, brahmacharya, aparigraha.
- ✅ Anekantavada and syadvada are Jain doctrines of the many-sidedness and conditional predication of truth (*syat* = "in some respect / from a standpoint"), distinct from moral relativism.
- ✅ The Kautilya passages the Commission quotes are from the *Arthasastra*'s chapter on inspecting the work of officers — Kangle's numbering 2.9.32–34.

### A11. Advanced traps

- ❌ Anekantavada is a form of ethical relativism ("nothing is really right or wrong"). → It is an epistemological doctrine about the complexity of truth-claims, not a denial of moral standards.
- ❌ Kautilya's *Arthasastra* advocates unrestrained state surveillance of all officials as a first resort. → His remedy pairs vigilance with positive incentives — permanence in office, rewards — for officials who serve with integrity. It is a balanced, not purely punitive, design.
- ❌ Nishkama karma means indifference to outcomes or results in public service. → It means non-attachment to personal credit or reward for the outcome, not indifference to whether the public duty is performed well.

### A12. Advanced PYQ-based analytical application

- ✅ The 2025 GS-IV Q4(b) demand on Mahavir and the Q5(a) demand on devotion to duty together test whether a candidate can move from a classical or religious teaching to a specific *contemporary* administrative relevance claim — exactly the translation exercise this topic emphasises. ⚠️ Both are routed elsewhere (Q4 to Topics 04 and 06; Q5 to Topics 09 and 04) and are used here as cross-topic anchors.
- ⚠️ Where a quotation question pairs two thinkers implicitly — Vivekananda's law-versus-morality claim alongside a code-of-conduct question — an advanced answer should explicitly connect the two registers rather than answering them in isolation.

### A13. Advanced Mains framework

**Central thesis:** Leader-based teachings function in GS-IV as *compressed ethical frameworks*; the examiner rewards their translation into a specific, boundary-aware administrative rule, not their recitation.

1. State the teaching precisely, verbatim where quoted.
2. Identify which values–institutions tension it addresses.
3. Translate it into one specific administrative rule or decision procedure.
4. Give a concrete governance example where the rule was or could be applied.
5. State the rule's own limitation, to demonstrate structural rather than merely rhetorical understanding.

### A14. Advanced probable questions

- ⚠️ **Prelims:** Which classical Indian text is cited by the Second ARC for an early structural — rather than purely moral — theory of official corruption?
- ⚠️ **Mains (10 marks):** Reconcile Kautilya's assumption that temptation is near-universal with Gandhi's emphasis on character. What does this imply for vigilance-system design?
- ⚠️ **Extended analytical practice (15 marks, not a GS-IV format):** "The strength of a society is not in its laws, but in the morality of its people." Does this understate the Second ARC's own institutional diagnosis of corruption in India?

### A15. Advanced study links

- ✅ Foundation companion: `basic/02_Human-Values-and-Lessons-from-Leaders.md`.
- ✅ `01_Ethics-and-Human-Interface.md` — the values-institutions synthesis this file builds on, and the Comprehensive National Power mechanism in full.
- ✅ `03_Attitude-Content-Structure-and-Persuasion.md` — the William James attitude-change link.
- ✅ `06_Indian-Moral-Thinkers-and-Philosophers.md` — Kautilya, Gandhi, Vivekananda and Buddha in depth.
- ✅ `21_Protecting-Honest-Officials-and-Vigilance-Administration.md` — the surveillance-versus-trust boundary this file flags.
- ✅ `Philosophy/paper-1/indian/Jainism.md` — anekantavada and syadvada in full philosophical depth.

### A16. Routed-demand record retained from the canonical owners

⚠️ Both canonical owners carry generated PYQ-integration blocks. Their content is reproduced in the ownership ledger above with the routing corrected against the verified ledgers. The owners' historical block records three routed demands for 2020 and 2022 — 2020 GS-IV Q1, 2020 GS-IV Q5 and 2022 GS-IV Q3 — and the 2024–2025 block records two, 2024 GS-IV Q2 and 2024 GS-IV Q6. ⚠️ The Basic owner additionally discusses the 2019 GS-IV Q6(c) Kalam demand as "routed here"; the verified ledger routes 2019 GS-IV Q6 to Topics 06 and 07, so this package treats it as a cross-topic anchor and records the divergence rather than resolving it silently.

|        vs INSTRUMENTAL (mode of conduct: honesty, punctuality, courage, impartiality)            |
|        USE -> an answer listing only instrumental values has not said what they are FOR.         |
| CUT 2  WIDENING CIRCLES: personal -> family -> social/community -> professional ->               |
|        constitutional.  USE -> GS-IV Section B conflicts are clashes BETWEEN circles.            |
| CUT 3  INTRINSIC (dignity: balanced only against another intrinsic claim)                        |
|        vs EXTRINSIC (efficiency: tradeable against a competing gain).                            |
| CUT 4  ESPOUSED (what the organisation prints) vs ENACTED (what it rewards).                     |
|        USE -> the gap between the two IS the diagnosis in most integrity questions.              |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| CONSTITUTIONAL CIRCLE IS TEXTUAL, NOT A MATTER OF OPINION:                                       |
|   51A(b) cherish and follow the noble ideals that inspired the national struggle for             |
|          freedom                                   <- THE EXEMPLAR DUTY                          |
|   51A(e) promote harmony and the spirit of common brotherhood ... transcending                   |
|          religious, linguistic and regional or sectional diversities; renounce                   |
|          practices derogatory to the dignity of women                                            |
|   51A(h) develop scientific temper, humanism and the spirit of inquiry and reform                |
|   51A(j) strive towards excellence in all spheres of individual and collective activity          |
|   51A(k) provide opportunities for education to his child or ward aged 6-14                      |
+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 6/40: FIVE TRANSMISSION MECHANISMS - AND THE FAILURE MODE OF EACH
+-- [04] FIVE TRANSMISSION MECHANISMS - AND THE FAILURE MODE OF EACH ------------------------------+
| 1 MODELLING       copied from an observed actor; 'what is done here' beats 'what is              |
|                   said here'.  ARC PREFACE (Confucius): the ruler who governs by his             |
|                   virtues 'may be compared to the Pole star which keeps its place and            |
|                   all other stars turn towards it'.                                              |
|                   FAILS WHEN: the model is unrepresentative or copied selectively.               |
| 2 REINFORCEMENT   conduct that is rewarded is repeated (incentives, appraisal,                   |
|                   promotion, stigma).  FAILS WHEN: espoused != rewarded (Cut 4).                 |
| 3 IDENTIFICATION  'people like us behave like this'.  ARC Annexure-I(2), p.217:                  |
|                   dismissal 'means disgrace for the entire family, and therefore,                |
|                   members of the family have an incentive to discourage corruption'.             |
|                   FAILS WHEN: the reference group's own norm is corrupt.                         |
| 4 INSTRUCTION     explicit teaching of a standard and its reasons (curriculum, code,             |
|                   iGOT module).  WEAKEST ALONE.  FAILS WHEN: taught as recall.                   |
| 5 REFLECTIVE      inherited values tested against experience.  ARC BOX 2.7                       |
|   REVISION        (Vivekananda): 'as knowledge comes, person grows, morality is                  |
|                   evolved, and idea of non-separateness begins.'                                 |
|                   FAILS WHEN: revision without an anchor = rationalised convenience.             |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| DIAGNOSTIC RULE -> name WHICH mechanism failed. A modelling failure is not cured by a            |
| curriculum; a reinforcement failure is not cured by a website statement.                         |
+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

+-- [05] LIMB B(i) - FAMILY: FIRST SCHOOL, LEAST REGULABLE INSTITUTION ----------------------------+
| FOUR CHANNELS                         RISK WHEN INVERTED                                         |
| 1 earliest modelling (pre-verbal)     models deceit as competence                                |
| 2 reputational identification         shields wrongdoing to protect family honour                |
|   [ARC: dismissal = disgrace for the  (LOYALTY CAPTURE = the first school of nepotism)           |
|   entire family; a bar on later                                                                  |
|   private employment raises the cost]                                                            |
| 3 allocation of opportunity           reproduces caste and gender hierarchy                      |
|   (who eats first, who is schooled)                                                              |
| 4 constitutionally named duty         NON-JUSTICIABLE; no legal sanction                         |
|   Art. 51A(k); also 51A(e)                                                                       |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| THE PARADOX -> strongest transmitter, least regulable institution. The state cannot              |
| script the content; it can set the CONDITIONS: (i) make office prestigious and                   |
| dismissal credible, which activates the ARC's reputational mechanism; (ii) guarantee             |
| the Art. 21A entitlement so schooling a girl is not a household decision alone;                  |
| (iii) remove the material barriers - distance, escort, toilets, cost of the uniform.             |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| MECHANISM CONDITION -> the ARC's family incentive works ONLY where office carries                |
| prestige AND dismissal is credible. Slow or reversible sanction flips the incentive.             |
+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
