ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 11/40: AFTER FORMATION - INTERNALISATION, PERFORMANCE AND FOUR CORROSION PATHS
+-- [08] AFTER FORMATION - INTERNALISATION, PERFORMANCE AND FOUR CORROSION PATHS ------------------+
| THE OBSERVABILITY TEST                                                                           |
|   unobserved AND conduct unchanged        -> INTERNALISED (robust under discretion)              |
|   unobserved AND conduct changes          -> PERFORMANCE (display for appraisal,                 |
|                                              publicity or fear of sanction)                      |
|   observed                                -> UNINFORMATIVE: both look identical                  |
| STRUCTURAL CONSEQUENCE -> any system that relies on observation can only ever detect             |
| PERFORMANCE. This is why vigilance cannot manufacture integrity.                                 |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| FOUR CORROSION PATHWAYS            MATCHING COUNTER-MOVE                                         |
| 1 NORMALISATION 'everyone does it'  make the deviation visible and countable                     |
|   [ARC 1.3 records this as the                                                                   |
|    prevailing Indian condition]                                                                  |
| 2 INCREMENTALISM small steps       declare a BRIGHT LINE (gift ceiling, mandatory                |
|                                     recusal trigger): converts gradient -> threshold             |
| 3 EUPHEMISM 'facilitation',        restore ACCURATE NAMING in the record: the                    |
|   'adjustment', 'courtesy'          prohibition was attached to the honest word                  |
| 4 DIFFUSED AGENCY 'I only signed'  fix PERSONAL ATTRIBUTION: one named officer per               |
|                                     decision, with recorded reasons                              |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| PROXIES, NOT PROOF -> conduct under low observability; consistency across differently            |
| placed beneficiaries; willingness to record a reason a hostile reader will see.                  |
| TRAP -> a completed training module or a signed integrity pledge is exactly what                 |
| PERFORMANCE produces.                                                                            |
+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                                  |
                                                  v
ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 12/40: USING VALUES IN A DECISION - CONFLICT, HIERARCHY AND THE TRADE-OFF (part 1 of 2)
+-- [09] USING VALUES IN A DECISION - CONFLICT, HIERARCHY AND THE TRADE-OFF (PART 1 OF 2) ---------+
| TWO SHAPES:                                                                                      |
|   TYPE A CIRCLE vs CIRCLE   family loyalty vs impartiality; community custom vs                  |
|                             constitutional equality; conscience vs lawful order                  |
|   TYPE B CLAIM vs CLAIM     transparency vs privacy; speed vs due process;                       |
|                             compassion vs equal treatment; many vs the rights of few             |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| FOUR-STEP RESOLUTION SPINE:                                                                      |
|   1 NAME BOTH VALUES PRECISELY - not 'a dilemma', say WHICH two                                  |
|   2 APPLY THE LEXICAL FILTER IN ORDER: (i) constitutional value / fundamental right              |
|     (ii) binding statute or rule (iii) institutional mandate and code                            |
|     (iv) professional norm (v) personal conscience                                               |
|     -> a lower tier NEVER overrides a higher one; it chooses among options the                   |
|        higher tier leaves open. Where conscience cannot: recorded dissent, lawful                |
|        escalation or resignation - never quiet non-compliance.                                   |
|   3 SEEK THE OPTION THAT DISSOLVES THE CLASH BEFORE TRADING: redact and disclose;                |
|     interim order with a short-dated review; grant relief AND record it as a CLASS               |
|     rule so the next similarly placed claimant also receives it                                  |
|   4 IF A TRADE-OFF IS UNAVOIDABLE: state the THRESHOLD crossed, the SAFEGUARD                    |
|     attached and the RESIDUAL RISK accepted                                                      |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| DIRECTION-SETTING AUTHORITY [ARC 2.2.2]: 'all persons holding authority derive it from           |
| the people ... all public functionaries are trustees of the people', and authority must          |
| be exercised 'in the best interest of the people or in "public interest"'.                       |
| -> A PRIVATE CLAIM IS DISQUALIFIED, NOT MERELY OUTWEIGHED. Hence RECUSAL, not careful            |
|    personal balancing.                                                                           |
| LOSES MARKS -> 'a balance must be struck between the two': no threshold, no safeguard,           |
+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 14/40: LIMB A - THE EXEMPLAR METHOD BEFORE ANY EXEMPLAR
+-- [10] LIMB A - THE EXEMPLAR METHOD BEFORE ANY EXEMPLAR -----------------------------------------+
| FIVE-SLOT TEMPLATE (every leader must fill ALL FIVE):                                            |
|   1 VALUE at stake  2 ACTION or citable TEXT  3 CONTEXT and when                                 |
|   4 ADMINISTRATIVE LESSON that follows TODAY  5 LIMITATION / TRANSFER CONDITION                  |
|   -> a leader filling only slot 2 is a scrapbook entry, not an examinable exemplar.              |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| PROVENANCE LADDER - attach ONE label to every quotation:                                         |
|   P1 READ IN A PRIMARY/OFFICIAL TEXT HELD LOCALLY   -> cite it                                   |
|      e.g. the five Arthasastra propositions, ARC Annexure-I(2), pp.214-215                       |
|   P2 ATTRIBUTED BY AN OFFICIAL REPORT, NO CITATION  -> 'as the ARC prints it'                    |
|      e.g. Box 2.8 Seven Social Sins; Preface 'Be the change you wish to see'                     |
|   P3 PRINTED BY UPSC, UNTRACEABLE                   -> 'as set by UPSC'                          |
|      e.g. 2024 Patel line; 2025 William James line; 2025 Vivekananda law/morality                |
|   P4 WIDELY CIRCULATED, NO TRACEABLE SOURCE         -> 'widely attributed to'                    |
|      e.g. the Potter Stewart line; the Dalai Lama success/sacrifice line                         |
|   RULE -> ANSWER THE IDEA AT EVERY LEVEL. NEVER INVENT A CITATION AT ANY LEVEL.                  |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| THE EXEMPLAR FALLACY: 'admired person said X' ==> 'X is the right administrative rule'           |
|   INVALID. A life is evidence about what is POSSIBLE, not proof about what is                    |
|   INSTITUTIONALLY CORRECT.                                                                       |
| THREE FAILURE MODES: selective quotation (name the institutional context) /                      |
| anachronism (diagnosis yes, institutional design no) / moral substitution (a quotation           |
| that could be deleted without weakening the argument was ornamental).                            |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| THE POSITIVE CASE, WHICH MUST ALSO BE MADE: exemplars supply a MEMORABLE and PORTABLE            |
| standard, CREDIBILITY (a real person kept it under pressure), and MORAL IMAGINATION              |
| beyond the current rulebook. Art. 51A(b) writes that function into the Constitution.             |
+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

+-- [13] EXEMPLAR 3 - KAUTILYA: THE FIVE PROPOSITIONS AND THE ASYMMETRIC REMEDY (PART 1 OF 2) -----+
| SOURCE READ DIRECTLY: ARC Annexure-I(2), National Colloquium speech of Chairman                  |
| M. Veerappa Moily, 1 SEPTEMBER 2006, report pp.214-215. The ARC spells it 'Arthasastra'          |
| and dates it to 'the Maurya era'. Kangle numbering for the first three: 2.9.32-2.9.34.           |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| DIAGNOSTIC [1] HONEY ON THE TONGUE - 'Just as it is not possible not to taste honey or           |
|   poison placed on the surface of the tongue, even so it is not possible for one                 |
|   dealing with the money of the king not to taste the money in however small a                   |
|   quantity.'                                        -> CUSTODY CREATES TEMPTATION                |
| DIAGNOSTIC [2] FISH DRINKING WATER - 'Just as fish moving inside water cannot be known           |
|   when drinking water, even so officers appointed for carrying out works cannot be               |
|   known when appropriating money.'                  -> THE OFFENCE IS UNOBSERVABLE               |
| DIAGNOSTIC [3] PATH OF BIRDS - 'It is possible to know even the path of birds flying             |
|   in the sky, but not the ways of officers moving with their intentions concealed.'              |
|                                                      -> INTENT IS OPAQUE                         |
| REMEDY [4] DISGORGE AND ROTATE - 'he should make those who have amassed (money                   |
|   wrongfully) yield it up and should change them in (their) works'                               |
| REMEDY [5] PERMANENCE FOR THE HONEST - 'those who do not consume (the king's) goods              |
|   and increase them in just ways, should be made permanent in their offices'                     |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| SHAPE OF THE ARGUMENT -> 3 DIAGNOSTIC + 2 PRESCRIPTIVE, and the two prescriptions are            |
| DELIBERATELY ASYMMETRIC: recovery + reassignment for the dishonest, SECURITY OF TENURE           |
| for the honest. QUOTING ONLY [1] REPORTS A CYNICISM AND MISSES THE DESIGN.                       |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| STRUCTURAL, NOT MORAL -> causation is in the situation (custody, unobservability,                |
| opacity), roughly two millennia before modern institutional economics.                           |
| LIMITATION -> [1] alone licenses pervasive surveillance whose Indian cost is                     |

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 21/40: EXEMPLAR 4 - MAHAVIR: VOWS PLUS AN ADMINISTRATIVE EPISTEMOLOGY (part 1 of 2)
+-- [14] EXEMPLAR 4 - MAHAVIR: VOWS PLUS AN ADMINISTRATIVE EPISTEMOLOGY (PART 1 OF 2) -------------+
| FIVE MAHAVRATAS            ADMINISTRATIVE TRANSLATION                                            |
|   AHIMSA (non-injury)      minimum-harm and proportionality test in enforcement,                 |
|                            eviction and crowd control                                            |
|   SATYA (truthfulness)     accurate reporting; refusal to improve a figure                       |
|   ASTEYA (non-stealing)    no appropriation of public resource, public time or credit            |
|   BRAHMACHARYA (restraint) discipline over appetite in a position of advantage                   |
|   APARIGRAHA (non-        conflict-of-interest and anti-nepotism norms; office is                |
|     possessiveness)        HELD, NOT OWNED  <- the ethical core of probity                       |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| ANEKANTAVADA (many-sidedness): reality is many-sided; no single standpoint captures it           |
|   whole.  -> consultation is an EPISTEMIC REQUIREMENT (you are missing information),             |
|   not a courtesy and not box-ticking.                                                            |
| SYADVADA (conditional predication): every assertion prefixed by SYAT, 'in some respect           |
|   / from a standpoint'.  -> STATE THE STANDPOINT OF EVERY FINDING. 'The project is               |
|   viable' -> 'viable on the revenue projection at current tariffs, not viable on the             |
|   displacement-cost estimate.'  A note that states its standpoints is AUDITABLE.                 |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| PRECISION POINT -> Jain tradition contrasts PARSHVANATHA's FOUR restraints with                  |
| MAHAVIR's FIVE. Brahmacharya is the vow Mahavir's tradition SEPARATELY ENUMERATED,               |
| having earlier been understood within aparigraha - NOT an ethical idea he is                     |
| documented to have INVENTED.                                                                     |
| DECISIVE TRAP -> anekantavada != moral relativism. It is a claim about the COMPLEXITY            |
| OF TRUTH-CLAIMS, not a denial that some acts are wrong, and NOT a licence to defer.              |
| TRANSFER CONDITION -> BOUNDED consultation: a stated closing date, a published record            |
| of what was heard, and a reasoned statement of which representations changed the                 |
| decision. Without these three, many-sidedness becomes a QUEUE OF VETOES.                         |

+-- [15] EXEMPLAR 5 - AMBEDKAR: CONSTITUTIONAL MORALITY AGAINST GRADED INEQUALITY (PART 1 OF 2) ---+
| METHOD NOTE -> the canonical owner deliberately STATES THE DOCTRINE rather than                  |
| attaching an unverified internet quotation. That editorial choice IS the Session-10              |
| lesson applied.                                                                                  |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| DIAGNOSIS: GRADED INEQUALITY - inequality is not a single line but an ASCENDING SCALE            |
|   OF REVERENCE and a DESCENDING SCALE OF CONTEMPT, in which each stratum has someone             |
|   below it -> THE EXCLUDED DO NOT AUTOMATICALLY COMBINE.                                         |
|   ADMINISTRATIVE CONSEQUENCE -> a scheme designed for 'the poor' can be captured by the          |
|   least excluded among them. DISAGGREGATE; do not assume solidarity.                             |
| INSTRUMENT: CONSTITUTIONAL MORALITY - disciplined fidelity to constitutional values and          |
|   lawful procedure rather than to personal preference, majority sentiment or official            |
|   convenience. Content: equality before law, dignity, liberty, fair procedure,                   |
|   accountability - applied precisely when locally unpopular.                                     |
| SOCIAL DEMOCRACY -> political democracy without social democracy is a 'top dressing';            |
|   formal legal equality is the FLOOR, not the achievement.                                       |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| THE OPERATIONAL TEST -> take the procedure that LOOKS NEUTRAL and ask whether its                |
| EFFECTS reproduce the existing hierarchy. Examples: a tender condition requiring three           |
| years of prior supply experience; a hearing fixed at district HQ at 11 a.m.                      |
| RULE -> PERSONAL BENEVOLENCE CANNOT SUBSTITUTE FOR INSTITUTIONAL JUSTICE.                        |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| CROSS-TOPIC ANCHOR: 2025 GS-IV Q1(b) premise - 'Constitutional morality is not a natural         |
| sentiment but a product of civil education and adherance of the rule of law' (printed            |
| spelling preserved). ROUTED TO TOPICS 13 AND 14 - not claimed here - but its premise IS          |
| Limb B stated in constitutional language.                                                        |
| LIMITATION -> constitutional morality FRAMES conflicts between two constitutional values         |
+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

+-- [16] EXEMPLAR 6 - SAVITRIBAI PHULE: BUILD THE MISSING INSTITUTION (PART 1 OF 2) ---------------+
| THE PROBLEM IS SELF-REPRODUCING, NOT ATTITUDINAL:                                                |
|   denial of girls' education + child marriage + caste-linked exclusion + widow stigma            |
|   -> each element makes the others RATIONAL from a household's point of view.                    |
|   (if a girl will marry early and not earn, schooling looks like a cost with no return;          |
|    if she is unschooled, early marriage looks like the only available security)                  |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| TWO POSSIBLE RESPONSES:                                                                          |
|   A EXHORTATION       condemn the practice, wait for attitudes -> system intact                  |
|   B INSTITUTION-      build the thing that does not exist -> CHANGES THE HOUSEHOLD'S             |
|     BUILDING          ACTUAL OPTION SET                                                          |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| VERIFIED FACTS [canonical Basic owner]:                                                          |
|   * with JYOTIRAO PHULE, co-founded India's first girls' school at BHIDE WADA, PUNE,             |
|     in 1848                                                                                      |
|   * faced social hostility for teaching girls                                                    |
|   * later worked on widow-remarriage support, and against child marriage and caste               |
|     discrimination                                                                               |
|   * conventionally regarded as India's first woman teacher/headmistress in the modern            |
|     sense                                                                                        |
| SOURCE LIMITATION, STATED -> the local OCR-searchable books contain NO substantive               |
| passage on her; a full-text scan returns only Bipan Chandra p.86 naming JYOTIBA Phule            |
| with Narayana Guru as 'two unrelenting critics of the caste system'. NO QUOTATION IS             |
| ATTRIBUTED TO HER IN THIS PACKAGE, because none is locally verifiable.                           |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| LESSON -> structural exclusion is corrected by BUILDING THE MISSING INSTITUTION, not by          |
| condemning the attitude the missing institution produced.                                        |
+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

|     expensive and unreliable at scale                                                            |

|   RULES DECIDE HOW LARGE THAT SPACE IS.                                                          |
| THE THIRD TERM MOST ANSWERS OMIT -> THE EXEMPLAR supplies CREDIBILITY: a demonstration           |
| that the standard has been kept by a real person under real pressure. Neither a code nor         |
| a sanction can do this. Art. 51A(b) is the constitutional acknowledgement.                       |

+-- [23] ANSWER SPINE AND QUALIFIED VERDICT (PART 1 OF 2) -----------------------------------------+
| ARCHITECTURE 1 - QUOTATION ANSWER (10 marks / 150 words)                                         |
|   restate the idea (~20w) -> thinker's context + attribution caution IN ONE CLAUSE               |
|   (~20w) -> NAMED ADMINISTRATIVE MECHANISM (~45w) -> ONE CONCRETE INDIAN EXAMPLE                 |
|   (~40w) -> one-line limit + takeaway (~25w).   BIOGRAPHY GETS ZERO WORDS.                       |
| ARCHITECTURE 2 - INCULCATION ANSWER (10 marks / 150 words)                                       |
|   define the transmission problem -> NAME THE MECHANISM for each institution asked               |
|   about -> ONE NAMED TEXT OR FINDING each (family: the ARC disgrace incentive and                |
|   51A(k); society: Cooter's everyday-norms finding; school: Art. 21A, 51A(b)/(h),                |
|   ARC 2.7.2) -> the FAILURE MODE of each -> qualified verdict with the generational              |
|   horizon.                                                                                       |
| ARCHITECTURE 3 - CASE STUDY (20 marks / 250 words) [architecture owned by Topic 22]              |

### The two printed limbs and why they are one argument

- Official clause: *"Human Values- lessons from the lives and teachings of great leaders, reformers and administrators; role of family society and educational institutions in inculcating values."*
- **Limb A** exemplar; **Limb B** inculcation. A value sits above attitude and below codified law, so it must be **formed** (Limb B, because law cannot form it) and **modelled** (Limb A, because an unkept standard is not believed).
- Article **51A(b)** — the duty to cherish and follow the noble ideals that inspired the national struggle — is the constitutional bridge between the two limbs.

### Definition and the five-level ladder

- **Value** = enduring, trans-situational, evaluative standard. Endurance excludes a mood; trans-situationality excludes an attitude; evaluation excludes a mere description.
- Ladder: belief/feeling → attitude (object-specific, Topic 03) → **value** → norm (shared expectation, stigma) → law/rule/code (enforceable, sanction).
- Adjacent terms: norm, virtue (settled excellence), interest (what benefits me), aptitude (capacity, Topic 04).
- ARC **2.2.1** — values upstream: "Ethics provides the basis for the creation of such laws and rules ... Our legal system emanates from a shared vision of what is good and just."
- ARC **2.2.4** — values downstream: rules "cannot provide for all situations"; "the higher the echelon in public service, the greater is the ambit of discretion."
- Preface: "One cannot mandate honesty. The rule of law can only defeat the perverse mind. However, it cannot defeat the perversity of the heart."

### Conceptual origin and minimum chronology

- Conceptual origin: *ethikos* = "arising from habit" (ARC 1.2). Minimum dating rule: a classical teacher diagnosed an incentive problem; he did not design a modern institution.
- 1869–1920 piecemeal prohibitions (lending 1869, gifts 1876, property 1881, investments 1885, promoting companies 1885, post-retirement employment 1920) → 1930s compendium → 1947 Prevention of Corruption Act → 1955 distinct rules → **1964 Santhanam Committee** and CCS (Conduct) Rules 1964 → **1976 42nd Amendment**, Part IVA, Article 51A, ten duties (Swaran Singh Committee) → **2002 86th Amendment**, Article 21A plus the eleventh duty 51A(k) → **1 September 2006** National Colloquium and Annexure-I(2) → draft Public Service Bill 2006 → **January 2007** ARC 4th Report → **2 September 2020** Mission Karmayogi → **1 April 2021** Capacity Building Commission → **6 February 2026** dated figures.
- Trajectory to quote: the state moved steadily **upstream** because each stage arrived too late.

### The four cuts through "value"

- Terminal (justice, dignity, welfare) vs instrumental (honesty, punctuality, impartiality) — an instrumental-only list has not said what it is *for*.
- Widening circles: personal → family → social → professional → constitutional. Section-B conflicts are clashes **between** circles.
- Intrinsic (dignity: balanced only against another intrinsic claim) vs extrinsic (efficiency: tradeable).
- **Espoused vs enacted** — the diagnostic move in almost every integrity question.

### Five transmission mechanisms and their failure modes

| Mechanism | How it transmits | Primary anchor | Fails when |
|---|---|---|---|
| Modelling | copied from an observed actor | ARC Preface, Confucius: the virtuous ruler is "compared to the Pole star" | the model is unrepresentative or copied selectively |
| Reinforcement | rewarded conduct is repeated | espoused–enacted cut | the printed value is not the rewarded one |
| Identification | "people like us behave like this" | ARC Annexure-I(2): dismissal "means disgrace for the entire family" | the reference group's norm is itself corrupt |
| Instruction | explicit teaching of a standard | ARC 2.7.2 on post-entry difficulty | taught as recall; weakest alone |
| Reflective revision | inherited values tested against experience | ARC Box 2.7, Vivekananda: "morality is evolved" | revision without an anchor becomes rationalised convenience |

- Diagnostic rule: name the failing mechanism. A modelling failure is not cured by a curriculum.

### Family — channels, conditions and inversions

- Channels: earliest modelling; reputational identification; allocation of opportunity; the constitutionally named duty at 51A(k) and 51A(e).
- ARC mechanism, verbatim sense: where office "is highly regarded and brings prestige, dismissal means disgrace for the entire family, and therefore, members of the family have an incentive to discourage corruption"; a bar on later private-sector employment raises the cost further.
- **Mechanism condition:** works only where office carries prestige **and** dismissal is credible.
- Inversions: modelled acquisitiveness; loyalty capture (nepotism, dowry pressure); reproduced hierarchy in household allocation.
- State levers: prestige plus credible sanction; the Article 21A entitlement; removal of material barriers (distance, escort, toilets, uniform cost).

### Society — the mechanism and its reverse

- Cooter, reported in Annexure-I(2): "where people freely interact on a repeated basis, they are more likely to form strong and legitimate norms"; "most citizens judge civil servants by social norms learned in these everyday interactions."
- Associations as **"law merchants"** in low-corruption countries — promulgating codes of good practice and imposing anti-corruption sanctions.
- Reverse direction, ARC 1.3: interventions "seen as mere posturing"; corruption regarded as "inevitable" and effort as "futile"; cynicism "bodes ill for our democratic system itself."
- Transmitters: community, religion/tradition (durable; requires a public-reason boundary — Article 51A(e)), peer group/cadre (strongest for a serving adult), media (sets salience).
- Payoff line: the tehsil counter is the classroom; service-delivery quality is itself a value-transmission instrument.

### Educational institutions — three layers and a legal floor

- Layers: explicit curriculum (weakest alone) → pedagogy and school practice → **hidden curriculum** (seating, cleaning duty, language correction, whether the mid-day meal is eaten together) which dominates.
- Legal floor, both from the **86th Amendment, 2002**: Article **21A** (justiciable fundamental right) and Article **51A(k)** (non-justiciable parental duty). Also 51A(h) — scientific temper, humanism, spirit of inquiry and reform — and 51A(b), the exemplar duty.
- Santhanam 1964, quoted at ARC 2.7.1: find "ways and means ... to ensure that idealism and patriotism have the proper place in the ambition of our youth"; "the lack of moral earnestness ... is perhaps the greatest single factor which hampers the growth of strong traditions of integrity and efficiency."
- **ARC 2.7.2**, the governing quotation: inculcation of values enabling "subordination of the self to a larger, societal good" and "a spirit of empathy for those in need of ameliorative state interventions" are "not skills which could be easily imbibed after joining the civil services ... need nurturing over not merely individual life-times, but through successive generations - the 'right' ethos takes long to evolve."
- Both edges: *for* school-stage formation; *against* promising results in one policy cycle. Current vehicle: NEP 2020 (text not held locally; no clause quoted).
- Honest limit: schools reproduce inequality where enrolment, retention, language and teacher attention track caste, gender and income.

### Internalisation, performance and corrosion

- Observability test: unobserved and unchanged = internalised; unobserved and changed = performance; observed = uninformative.
- Structural consequence: observation-based systems can only detect performance, which is why vigilance cannot manufacture integrity.
- Corrosion paths and counters: **normalisation** → visibility and counting; **incrementalism** → a declared bright line converting a gradient into a threshold; **euphemism** → accurate naming in the record; **diffused agency** → one named officer per decision with recorded reasons.
- Proxies, not proof: low-observability conduct; consistency across differently placed beneficiaries; willingness to record a reason a hostile reader will see.

### Value conflict — the four-step resolution spine

1. Name both values precisely.
2. Apply the lexical filter: constitutional value / fundamental right → binding statute or rule → institutional mandate and code → professional norm → personal conscience. Where conscience cannot prevail: recorded dissent, lawful escalation or resignation, never quiet non-compliance.
3. Seek the dissolving option before trading: redaction; interim order with short-dated review; relief granted **and recorded as a class rule**.
4. If a trade-off is unavoidable: state the threshold crossed, the safeguard attached and the residual risk accepted.

- Direction-setting authority, ARC **2.2.2**: "all persons holding authority derive it from the people ... all public functionaries are trustees of the people", exercising authority "in the best interest of the people or in 'public interest'." A private claim is therefore **disqualified, not outweighed** — hence recusal.
- Loses marks: "a balance must be struck between the two."

### The exemplar method — template, provenance and fallacy

- Five slots per leader: value → action or citable text → context → administrative lesson → limitation and transfer condition.
- Provenance ladder: **P1** primary/official text read locally; **P2** attributed by an official report without citation; **P3** printed by UPSC but untraceable; **P4** widely circulated with no traceable source. Answer the idea at every level; never invent a citation.
- Exemplar fallacy: a life is evidence about what is **possible**, not proof about what is **institutionally correct**.
- Failure modes: quotation scrapbook (deletion test), selective quotation (name the context), anachronism (diagnosis not design), hero-worship, moral substitution (ARC 1.4), fabricated provenance.
- Positive case: exemplars supply memorability, credibility and moral imagination — recognised at Article 51A(b).

### Gandhi

- ARC **Box 2.8** heading verbatim: "The Seven Social Sins, as quoted by Mahatma Gandhi in 'Young India,' 1925". Items: politics without principles; wealth without work; leisure without conscience; knowledge without character; commerce without morality; science without humanity; worship without sacrifice.
- Structure: **one means–ends decoupling fault repeated seven times.** Read the column, not the list.
- Four ARC-printed anchors, all P2: the Preface epigraph on remaking ourselves rather than the world; the uncited "Be the change you wish to see in the world" (nearest documented theme, *Indian Opinion*, 1913); **Box 2.2** on corruption and hypocrisy as products of democracy; and Box 2.8.
- Textual variance: ARC prints "leisure"; *Young India*, 22 October 1925 (Collected Works Vol. XXVIII) reads "pleasure"; Gandhi introduced the list as a correspondent's. Formulation: **published and endorsed**.
- Trusteeship maps to ARC 2.2.2. Limitation: diagnostic without enforcement — pair with ARC 1.4's design approach. Transfer condition: restraint must become a **recordable act**.

### Vivekananda

- **Text A (P1):** the 1897 Lahore instruction, *Complete Works* Vol. III, "The Common Bases of Hinduism" — rule: **adaptation, not transplant**; preserve the mechanism, redesign the delivery. Double-edge: "adaptation" is also the vocabulary of dilution.
- **Text B (P1, inside the report):** ARC **Box 2.7** "Evolution of Morality" — "as knowledge comes, person grows, morality is evolved, and idea of non-separateness begins ... That is the foundation of morality. It is the quintessence of all ethics." A **theory of value formation**; Mechanism 5.
- **Text C (P3):** the 2025 law-versus-morality line, not traceable to the *Complete Works*; nearest documented theme, "Our Duty to the Masses", 23 June 1894. Substantive reply: strictly read it understates the ARC's structural diagnosis. Synthesis: law without internalised morality is under-enforced because enforcement needs honest enforcers; morality without law is unenforceable at scale.

### Kautilya

- Source: ARC **Annexure-I(2)**, Moily's National Colloquium speech, **1 September 2006**, report pages 214–215. ARC spelling "Arthasastra"; "Maurya era". Kangle 2.9.32–2.9.34 for the first three.
- Diagnosis: honey or poison on the tongue (custody creates temptation); fish drinking water (appropriation is unobservable); path of birds (intent is opaque).
- Remedy, deliberately **asymmetric**: those who amassed wrongfully must "yield it up" and be "change[d] ... in (their) works"; those who increase wealth "in just ways" must be "made permanent in their offices."
- Structural not moral. Limitation: proposition [1] alone licenses surveillance whose cost is decision-avoidance and harassment of record-keepers. Transfer condition: rotation must be **rule-bound, published and reviewable** or it becomes the punitive transfer. Anachronism caution: diagnosis and incentive logic yes; constitutional vigilance architecture no.

### Mahavir

- Five *Mahavratas* with translations: ahimsa (proportionality in enforcement), satya (accurate reporting), asteya (no appropriation of resource, time or credit), brahmacharya (restraint under advantage), **aparigraha** (conflict-of-interest and anti-nepotism; office held, not owned — the ethical core of probity).
- **Anekantavada** — many-sidedness → consultation as an epistemic requirement. **Syadvada** — *syat*, "from a standpoint" → state the standpoint of every finding, which makes a note auditable.
- Precision point: Parshvanatha's four restraints against Mahavir's five; brahmacharya **separately enumerated**, not invented.
- Decisive trap: anekantavada is not moral relativism and not a licence to defer. Transfer condition: bounded consultation — closing date, published record, reasoned response. Doctrinal depth: Topic 06 and the Philosophy Jainism file.

### Ambedkar

- Method note: state the **doctrine**, not an unverified quotation.
- Graded inequality: an ascending scale of reverence and a descending scale of contempt, so **the excluded do not automatically combine** — therefore disaggregate rather than assume solidarity.
- Constitutional morality: disciplined fidelity to constitutional values and lawful procedure rather than to personal preference, majority sentiment or official convenience. Political democracy without social democracy is a "top dressing".
- Operational test: does the facially neutral procedure reproduce the hierarchy in its effects? (Three-year prior-experience tender condition; a hearing fixed at district headquarters at 11 a.m.)
- Rule: **personal benevolence cannot substitute for institutional justice.** Cross-topic anchor: the 2025 GS-IV Q1(b) premise, routed to Topics 13 and 14. Limitation: frames conflicts between constitutional values, does not resolve them.

### Savitribai Phule

- Verified facts: with **Jyotirao Phule**, co-founded India's first girls' school at **Bhide Wada, Pune, in 1848**; faced documented social hostility; later widow-remarriage support and opposition to child marriage and caste discrimination; conventionally India's first modern woman teacher or headmistress.
- **Source limitation, stated:** the local OCR-searchable books contain no substantive passage on her; a full-text scan yields only Bipan Chandra page 86 naming *Jyotiba* Phule with Narayana Guru as "two unrelenting critics of the caste system". **No quotation is attributed to her in this package.**
- Problem structure: denial of girls' education + child marriage + caste-linked exclusion + widow stigma, each making the others rational for a household.
- Lesson: **build the missing institution** rather than exhort, because the option set changes behaviour more reliably than the opinion. Constitutional descendants: Articles 21A, 51A(k), 51A(e).
- Limitation: one institution is not a system. Transfer condition: the built institution must be **sustained and staffed**.

### Modern leaders and administrators, with provenance

| Exemplar | Teaching | Provenance | Administrative rule | Limit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Sardar Patel | faith and strength both essential (2024 Q3(b)) | P3, no primary source traced; printed artefact `Anslotte sarfre` | conviction × capacity, multiplicative | neither term alone delivers a public good |
| Thiruvalluvar | the untroubled trouble trouble itself (2025 Q3(a)) | modern paraphrase of **Tirukkural 623**, Ch. 63 *Iṭukkaṇ Aḻiyāmai*; G. U. Pope's rendering available | equanimity is operational — preserves assessment and communication | equanimity is not passivity |
| William James | life altered by altering attitudes (2025 Q3(b)) | P3, untraceable; popularised by Peale, 1952; genuine 1895 line available; Hindi text prints "William Jones" | attitudes are malleable, so adult training is not futile → Topic 03 | cannot dissolve structural constraint |
| A. P. J. Abdul Kalam (i) | righteousness → character → home → nation → world (2019 Q6(c)) | P2; Kalam credited it to Sathya Sai Baba, European Parliament, April 2007 | bottom-up theory of order; personal integrity is not merely private | a rhetorical chain, not a demonstrated causal claim; documented analogue is the ARC's Confucian Pole star |
| A. P. J. Abdul Kalam (ii) | a corruption-free nation through youth and education (2022 Q3(b)) | P3, UPSC paraphrase; theme echoed in *Indomitable Spirit* | generational, education-driven anti-corruption | complements rather than replaces enforcement |
| Potter Stewart | right to do vs right to do (2022 Q3(a)) | P4, no primary opinion, speech or writing traced | the legality–ethics gap | use ARC **Box 2.3** (Fulbright) as the documented version; Topic 10 owns the full treatment |
| Dalai Lama | judge success by what was given up (2022 Q3(c)) | P4, widely circulated public teaching | redefine administrative success as a net figure; record the cost | outcomes still matter; test whether what was surrendered was a value or a preference |

### The synthesis — character, system and the third term

- ARC **1.4** sets out both approaches: "overemphasis on values and character", whose implicit assumption is that "until values are restored, nothing much can be done"; and the incentive view that "if good behaviour is consistently rewarded and bad behaviour consistently punished, the bulk of the people follow the straight and narrow path."
- Character covers what the system cannot see (ARC 2.2.4; Kautilya's proposition [3]). System covers what character cannot sustain at scale (ARC 1.3).
- **Verdict line:** values decide conduct in the space rules cannot reach; rules decide how large that space is.
- **Third term:** the exemplar supplies **credibility** — a demonstration that the standard has been kept under real pressure — which neither a code nor a sanction can supply. Article 51A(b).
- Failure modes: character-first alone = moralising; design-first alone = surveillance.

### The four codified value texts — addressee and enforceability

| Text | Addressee | Content markers | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Article 51A | citizens | 51A(b) national-struggle ideals; (e) harmony and dignity of women; (h) scientific temper, humanism, inquiry and reform; (j) excellence; (k) education 6–14 | **non-justiciable**; no legal sanction; Parliament may legislate. Part IVA from the 42nd Amendment 1976 (ten duties, Swaran Singh Committee); eleventh from the 86th Amendment 2002. Rejected proposals: a penalty, immunity of the penalty law from challenge, a tax-paying duty |
| Draft Public Service Bill, 2006 (ARC 2.7.5) | civil servants | allegiance to the Preamble's ideals; apolitical functioning; good governance as primary goal; objectivity and impartiality; accountability and transparency; highest ethical standards; merit consistent with diversity; economy and avoidance of wastage; congenial work environment; communication, consultation, cooperation | **draft**, not enacted; ARC also envisaged a Public Service Code, a Management Code and a Public Service Authority |
| CCS (Conduct) Rules, 1964 | central civil servants | "maintaining integrity and absolute devotion to duty"; "conduct unbecoming of a government servant"; catalogue of proscribed acts | **enforceable**, but the ARC's January 2007 verdict is that they "do not constitute a Code of Ethics" and that "there is no Code of Ethics prescribed for civil servants in India" |
| Nolan principles (ARC 2.2.5) | holders of public office (UK) | first principle **Selflessness** — "should take decisions solely in terms of public interest" | the ARC's chosen international reference, described as "one of the most comprehensive statements" available |

- ARC **2.7.9** and **2.7.12(a)**: Public Service Values "should be stipulated by law", applicable to all tiers of Government and parastatal organisations, with "any transgression of these values ... treated as misconduct, inviting punishment." Model cited: the Australian Public Service Act 1999, under which the Public Service Commissioner evaluates agencies and reports annually to Parliament. **Status: recommended.**
- Indira Gandhi's stated justification for the duties: "the moral value of fundamental duties would be not to smoother rights but to establish a democratic balance by making the people conscious of their duties equally as they are conscious of their rights" (printed form `smoother` preserved).
- Exam move: never write "Indian values" — write which text, whom it binds, and whether it is enforceable.

### The value-education chain and Mission Karmayogi

- Chain: source → selection → formation → reinforcement → sanction → conduct under discretion.
- **Break A** selection cannot test internalisation. **Break B** instruction alone is weak (ARC 2.7.2). **Break C, decisive** the espoused value diverges from the rewarded conduct — no training budget defeats an appraisal form.
- ARC 1.1 requires disciplinary bodies that investigate **and** "impose sanctions quickly".
- Mission Karmayogi: Cabinet approval **2 September 2020**; FRAC; iGOT-Karmayogi; Capacity Building Commission **1 April 2021**; as on **6 February 2026** — 1.49 crore users, 4,342 courses, 7.26 crore course completions, 23 languages.
- Bounded verdict: **strong at the formation step, weak at the reinforcement step unless what is rewarded changes.** Reach is not internalisation; assert no general APAR linkage without a service-specific DoPT order naming scope.
- One high-leverage move without new legislation: grade "quality of recorded reasoning in discretionary decisions" in the appraisal, audited on a sample.

### Standing examiner traps

1. Defining a value by example ("honesty") instead of by structure (enduring, trans-situational).
2. Reversing value and attitude — the attitude is the object-specific item.
3. Treating Article 51A as enforceable; the justiciable education instrument is Article 21A.
4. Calling the CCS (Conduct) Rules India's code of ethics; the ARC's finding is the opposite.
5. Answering an inculcation question entirely with curriculum proposals — instruction is one of five mechanisms and the weakest alone.
6. Celebrating family values without the mirror image of nepotism, dowry pressure and gendered allocation.
7. Nostalgia about eroding social values, against ARC 1.3's record of an active corruption-tolerating norm.
8. Quoting only Kautilya's honey-and-poison line and omitting the permanence-for-the-honest proposition.
9. Reading anekantavada as relativism, or presenting brahmacharya as Mahavir's invention.
10. Reducing Ambedkar to "architect of the Constitution" instead of the graded-inequality test.
11. Attaching an invented quotation to Savitribai Phule, or answering with biography instead of the causal link.
12. Treating Kalam's righteousness chain as his own composition and as a demonstrated causal law.
13. Presenting the Potter Stewart line as a judicial dictum.
14. Citing Mission Karmayogi completions as evidence of improved ethics.
15. Claiming the 2024 and 2025 Q3 quotation questions as this topic's owned PYQs.
16. Writing a 15-mark GS-IV answer; GS-IV sets 10-mark/150-word Section-A items and 20-mark/250-word Section-B case studies only.

### PYQ anchors, ownership and the verified marks contract

- **Directly owned, verbatim-verified (4):** 2024 GS-IV Q2(a) peace as talk → belief → action with the weapon-industry premise; Q2(b) global warming as human greed in the name of development; Q6(a) equal opportunity irrespective of gender identity and the gender-specific challenges of female public servants; Q6(b) Mission Karmayogi and grassroots delivery.
- **Directly owned, neutral routed demands (5):** 2022 GS-IV Q3(a) Potter Stewart, Q3(b) Kalam on a corruption-free nation, Q3(c) Dalai Lama on success and sacrifice; 2020 GS-IV Q5(a) gender inequality and Savitribai Phule; 2020 GS-IV Q1(b) education as a pervasive tool with NEP 2020. The 2020 and 2022 papers are **not held locally**, so no verbatim claim is made.
- **Cross-topic anchors, not owned (7):** 2024 Q3(a) and Q3(b); 2025 Q3(a), Q3(b) and Q3(c); 2025 Q4(b); 2019 Q6(c). Routing: Topics 06 and 07, except 2025 Q4 which is routed to Topics 04 and 06.
- **Marks contract, read directly from both locally held papers:** Section A — 10 marks, "(Answer in 150 words)". Section B — 20 marks, "(Answer in 250 words)". **No 15-mark question exists in GS-IV.** Any 15-mark practice is labelled extended analytical practice.

### The answer spine

- **Quotation answer (150 words):** restate (~20w) → context plus attribution caution in one clause (~20w) → named administrative mechanism (~45w) → one concrete Indian example (~40w) → limit and takeaway (~25w). Biography gets nothing.
- **Inculcation answer (150 words):** define the transmission problem → name the mechanism per institution → one named text or finding each (family: the ARC disgrace incentive and 51A(k); society: Cooter's everyday-norms finding; school: Article 21A, 51A(b)/(h), ARC 2.7.2) → the failure mode of each → qualified verdict with the generational horizon.
- **Case study (250 words, architecture owned by Topic 22):** facts and assumptions → stakeholders → values in conflict → at least three options with consequences → lawful ethical decision → implementation → safeguard and residual risk. This topic supplies precise value naming, the lexical ordering, and an explicit statement of what the decision costs.
- **Evidence pool E1–E7, each with its limitation:** Gandhi's Seven Social Sins; Vivekananda's 1897 borrowing instruction; Bhide Wada 1848; Kalam's chain with the ARC's Confucian analogue; Tirukkural 623; Kautilya's five propositions; ARC 2.7.2 with Santhanam 1964.
- **Counterpoint template:** "This teaching is administratively valuable because [named mechanism], but its limitation is [X]; it should therefore be applied alongside [named institutional safeguard], not relied on alone."

### Source and attribution discipline

- ✅ read in a locally held primary or official text; ⚠️ analysis, inference or a stated attribution caution; 📰 dated current anchor.
- Provenance states: **P1** primary/official text read; **P2** official report's uncited attribution; **P3** UPSC's printed attribution, untraceable; **P4** widely circulated, no traceable source.
- P1 items in this topic: the five *Arthasastra* propositions; ARC Box 2.7; ARC Box 2.8's heading and list; ARC Box 2.2; ARC Box 2.3's Fulbright passage; ARC 1.1–1.4, 2.2.1–2.2.5 and 2.7.1–2.7.12; the Preface epigraph and the Confucius, Plato and Solzhenitsyn passages; Vivekananda's 1897 Lahore instruction; Articles 21A and 51A with their amendment history and non-justiciability; the 2024 and 2025 GS-IV printed wordings.
- P3/P4 items: the 2024 Patel line; the 2025 William James line; the 2025 Vivekananda law-versus-morality line; the Potter Stewart line; the Dalai Lama line; the Kalam corruption-free-nation wording.
- **No quotation is attributed to Savitribai Phule**, because none is locally verifiable.
- **Remaining limitations:** UPSC publishes no official descriptive model answers, so every model here is instructional. Only the 2024 and 2025 GS-IV papers are held locally, so the 2019, 2020 and 2022 routed demands remain neutral renderings. NEP 2020's text is not held locally and no clause of it is quoted. The locally held Ethics coaching compendium is an image-only scan and is not used. The claim that studying an admirable life predicts admirable conduct is **not** established by any source used here; exemplars are treated as inputs to conduct, not guarantees of it.

### The qualified conclusion

Human values are formed socially and remain revisable; they are transmitted by five mechanisms of which explicit instruction is the weakest; family, society and educational institutions each transmit and each can invert; leaders, reformers and administrators supply memorability, credibility and moral imagination but never a warrant for an inferred rule; and the operative administrative proposition is that **values decide conduct in the space rules cannot reach, while rules decide how large that space is** — which is why the Second ARC demands a culture of integrity and competent disciplinary machinery in the same sentence.
